Exodus shares rise 10% ahead of Q3 earnings showing 7% revenue growth, $8.3 million loss

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The wallet provider posted an $8.3 million net loss, swinging from $17 million in net income in Q3 2025.

Exodus shares rose 10.07% on Monday to close at $4.48, ahead of the company's third-quarter earnings release, which showed higher revenue but a return to quarterly losses.

The NYSE American-listed company's stock remained down 8.57% over five trading days and 70.21% since the start of 2026, according to The Block's EXOD price page.

Exodus reported $32.5 million in revenue for the three months ended Sept. 30, up 7% from $30.3 million a year earlier, the company said in a statement.

Net income attributable to the company swung to an $8.3 million loss from $17 million in profit in the third quarter of 2025. Adjusted EBITDA also turned negative, with a $3.9 million loss compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $5.9 million a year earlier.

"Q3 was about execution: integrating the organization, restoring some payment capabilities, sharpening our product roadmap, and building a more disciplined operating model," the company wrote on X.

Exodus reported $1.1 billion in gross transaction volume during the quarter, alongside $1.1 billion in total swap volume. It also reported 1.6 million active cards and 1.4 million monthly active users, according to the statement.

Quarterly funded users increased 7.7% from the previous quarter to 1.4 million, while swap volume declined 3.4% from Q2.

The company has been expanding beyond its self-custodial wallet business. In June, Exodus launched Exodus Markets with Ondo Finance, offering eligible customers access to more than 200 tokenized stocks, ETFs, and other assets through its wallet on Solana.

"We are building toward a financial system where anyone can receive money, invest it, and spend it as easily as they send a message, while retaining control of their assets," Exodus CEO JP Richardson said in the statement.

Meanwhile, the company said it remains on track to reduce combined annual costs by approximately $30 million going into 2027, up from an earlier $10 million to $13 million target as it integrates Monavate and Baanx.

10-Minute Full-Body Stretch for Dog Owners

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Longtime yoga teacher Amber Burke, graduate of two YogaWorks teacher trainings and former leader of a teacher training program at the University of New Mexico-Taos, shares her favorite stretches to do before walking her dogs. You can also explore other ways to stretch your entire body.

Confession time. I love my dogs, but I don’t always love walking them. Dog walking, I have found, bears little resemblance to normal walking. It is far more physically demanding than I ever imagined in my pre-dog-owning years, and sometimes it’s even a little scary. I have wrenched my back when my two big rescue mutts decided to dart in front of me while playing tug-of-war. I have been pulled after squirrels and tugged straight into lampposts. I have fallen into bushes. I have faceplanted. It is a small miracle that my shoulders are still attached to my body and I have (almost) all of my teeth.

Even when a dog walk goes smoothly, I usually end up feeling a bit crooked, hunched over, and frazzled by the end. If, like me, you walk big dogs, two or more dogs, high-strung or strong-willed dogs, and live anywhere with squirrels, you might value some yoga poses and movements that strengthen and stretch your shoulders, improve your balance, and stabilize your back, hips, and legs to help you survive the demands of your next dog walk.

This standing practice can be done in just a few minutes before your next dog walk, or simply do one or a few of these exercises while your dogs are busy running in the dog park. No mat or lowering yourself to the ground required. You will need a strap—or dog leash, after you’ve untangled yourself from it.

The top priority for dog walkers is shoulder stability. Dogs yank, and when your dogs go after a squirrel, you don’t want your shoulders to go with them. Train your shoulders to resist every yank with this variation of Mountain Pose (Tadasana).

How to: Stand tall with your feet hip-distance apart in Mountain Pose and your arms alongside your body, squeezing inward in at your sides. Bend your elbows. Roll your shoulders up and back, and then down your back. Draw your shoulder blades toward each other, imagining that you’re creating a crease between them. Pause here for several breaths, then release.

This exercise helps strengthen the muscles that keep your shoulder blades in place and reverses that forward-rounding back hunch that might result from holding a leash in front of you.

How to: Take a hold of a leash (or strap) behind your back with your palms facing forward, about shoulder-distance apart, or if your hands meet behind you, interlace your fingers behind you. Keeping your shoulders back and your arms straight, pulse your arms up and down several times, lifting them as high as is comfortable. Repeat for several rounds, then release.

Dog walking takes your arms—and your attention—forward and down as you keep an eye on your dog, any potential obstacles, and tempting trash items. This exercise moves your body in the opposite direction, broadening across your chest and stretching the fronts of your shoulders.

How to: Reach your arms straight in front of your chest. Hold a leash between your hands, taking them slightly wider apart than shoulder-width. As you inhale, lift the leash overhead and lower it slightly behind your shoulders; as you exhale, bring it back in front of you, keeping your arms relatively straight the entire time. (If your arms bend a lot, move your hands wider on the leash.) Do this for several rounds.

You can also pause with the leash behind you, if that gives you a good stretch, for a few breaths. Then lower your arms and release the leash.

If you have more than one dog, it’s a good idea to be ready for that cumbersome moment when they criss-cross their leashes, going in opposite directions. This stretch allows you to practice that arm position by stretching the muscles along the backs of your shoulders and in between your shoulder blades.

How to: Cross your arms at the elbows in front of your chest. Your arms will be slightly bent. Make soft fists, then reach your hands away from each other as if you are trying to straighten your arms in opposite directions. Pause here for a few breaths, then release your arms and switch sides so the opposite arm is on top.

It‘s all too easy to twist an ankle if your dog pulls you sideways while you’re stepping off a curb or if you’re simply paying more attention to your furry friend than your own footing. Heel lifts help to strengthen your ankles and your balance.

How to: Stand with your feet together or hip-distance apart and your arms by your sides. Gaze at a steady point on the ground or, for more of a balance challenge, at a fixed point on the horizon. Press down with the balls of your feet. As you inhale, lift your heels; as you exhale, slowly lower your heels, without letting the ankles splay to the sides. Do this several times.

When your dog jumps on you unexpectedly, the last thing you want to do is take a tumble. This dynamic pose variation helps you cultivate leg strength and balance to prepare you to stand strong.

How to: With your feet together or hip-distance apart, gaze at an unmoving point in front of you. Press down into the balls of your feet, then lift your heels. Bend your knees, lean your chest slightly forward, sink your hips toward the ground, and reach your arms straight out in front of your chest in a Chair Pose variation. For more of a challenge, bend your knees more deeply to bring your hips closer to the ground, and try to keep your posture more upright instead of leaning forward, drawing your belly toward your spine. Pause for several breaths, then lower your heels and arms as you return to standing.

If your dogs have ever tried to take off, you know how important it is to be able lower your center of gravity fast so you can resist their pull. Get ready to drop down into a sturdy position by practicing Squat (Malasana).

How to: Stand with your feet a little wider than hip-width and turn your toes out slightly. Bend your knees and lower your hips as far as you like, tracking your knees over your toes. Bring your elbows inside your knees and your hands together in prayer position in Squat. Broaden across your chest and lift your heart space. Stay here for a few breaths, then press your feet into the ground to rise back to standing.

Dog walkers do a lot of forward folding—we bend down to pet our dogs, to pick up after them, to take their harnesses on and off, and to pry things that they really shouldn’t be eating out of their mouths. Pyramid Pose (Parsvottanasana) warms up your hamstrings and mobilizes your lower back to help you do all that with greater ease.

How to: Press down through your left foot and step your right foot around 12 inches behind you. Place your hands on your hips. Hinge from your hips and fold your chest forward as much as is comfortable, or about parallel to the ground. Bend your front knee if you like. Stay in Pyramid Pose, petting a dog if you like, or drape your upper body over your front leg and let your head relax for more of a more intense stretch. To come out of the pose, walk your hands up your front leg. Then change sides.

Dog walkers need to be able to preserve their stability, and also change speeds and directions on the fly. You might need to dart sideways out of the way of another walker who isn’t paying attention, or quickly escape an approaching off-leash dog. Use this move to practice fast footwork and energize your legs.

How to: Standing with your feet together, hinge from your hips and reach your chest forward. Cross your right foot behind your left, planting the ball of your right foot as far outside your left foot as feels comfortable. Bend your left leg slightly. Reach your arms toward the left, then step your right foot back to the starting position. Then cross your left foot behind and outside your right foot and reach your arms toward the right. Move from side to side several times, building momentum and going as slowly or quickly as you want. Then return to standing.

The front of your thighs might be feeling the work of the previous poses—and months or years of dog walking. Dancer’s Pose (Natarajasana) provides a stretch for the quadriceps, as well as a backbend that gently counterstretches all the forward reaching and bending of dog walking. It also gives you another chance to strengthen your balance.

How to: Shift your weight into your left foot. Bend your right knee and draw your right heel toward your buttock. Loop your leash or strap around your right foot or ankle, draw the strap over your right shoulder, and hold it with one or both hands in Dancer Pose variation. Stay here for a few breaths, or use both hands to lift the strap overhead and press your right shin behind you, arching into a backbend. Draw your elbows toward each other and lift your chest. Lower down and change sides.

Do you find yourself holding onto the leash for dear life? All that gripping can create tightness in the hands and wrists. Here’s a way to relieve that tension.

How to: Reach your arms in front of your chest and interlace your fingers with your palms facing forward. Straighten your arms as you press your palms away from you. Stay here for several breaths, or lift your arms overhead to add a full-body stretch. Then release your hands and lower your arms by your sides.

In addition to stretching your back and hamstrings, Standing Forward Bend (Uttanasana) can also be mentally relaxing, allowing you to breathe out the stresses that come with daily life…and dog walking.

How to: Plant your feet hip-distance apart. Fold forward, walking your hands down your legs and bending your knees as much as you need. Place your hands on the ground or your shins in Standing Forward Bend, grasp opposite elbows, or let your arms hang toward the ground in Rag Doll. Relax your neck. Pause here for several breaths, then walk your hands up your legs to return to standing.

After you’ve returned home from your dog walk, give yourself several minutes of well-deserved rest. You made it!

How to: Recline on your yoga mat, with or without dogs. (Only you know if they will enhance your relaxation or distract you from it!) Sigh out any remaining tension. Allow your shoulders to soften toward the floor with your  hands out to the sides or resting on your body in  Savasana. You don’t need to be on guard anymore. Give yourself over to relaxation, the way dogs know how to do when the fun is over. For a few minutes, surrender your weight to the earth, and let yourself be a big warm animal, gently breathing.

Under pressure, Big Walk studio reveals it used generative AI "sparingly" while making its hit 2026 game

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

"It has become a matter of public interest."

Australian developer House House has revealed it used generative AI "sparingly" to help make celebrated, indie, multiplayer puzzle game Big Walk.

In a statement sent to Aftermath - following a judging fallout related to the Independent Games Festival - House House revealed it had used large language models primarily to help debug the game's code.

"In the final stretch of Big Walk's development, LLMs were used sparingly by House House to help work through difficult technical challenges and bugs," the developer said. "In some cases, snippets from LLM responses were copied as a starting point to be modified into game code - we'd estimate that well under 0.1 percent of Big Walk's code had anything to do with this usage, and generative AI was not used in any other context when making the game."

House House added: "The primary use case for House House's use of LLMs was to research, via chatbots, issues we encountered with third-party code."

The studio's revelation comes after a sizeable number of IGF jurors - 19 of them - issued a statement saying they would not vote for any game that used generative AI during its development. This was done after seeing "a significant increase" in entry submissions involving generative AI use, which "set off some fairly intense discussion and disagreement across the judge and jury pools". And rather than not vote for these in silence, a collection of jurors decided to take a public stand.

House House hadn't disclosed Big Walk generative AI use anywhere before disclosing it for its IGF submission. "We have not made any disclosure on Steam as the platform does not request disclosure of code or chatbot usage," the studio explained. "We are posting this disclosure as it has become a matter of public interest."

House House added: "This is not an endorsement of, or argument for, the use of AI tools, however minimal. We have some concern that voluntary disclosures like this might contribute to the normalisation of this technology, or its treatment as 'inevitable'. We support the work of those who are trying to limit the harm generative AI is doing to video games and our world, and apologise that Big Walk's LLM usage may not meet their standards.

"Big Walk was developed without an internal AI usage policy in place, and we consider that lack of policy a failure. We will work to have a more comprehensive policy going forwards."

It's a revelation that's likely to leave a taint of some degree on one of this year's brightest stars - a game Eurogamer awarded a full five stars to in its Big Walk review.

XRP Ledger Introduces Quantum-Proof Security Upgrade to Users

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

As the XRP Ledger developers continue to make committed efforts to make the network fully quantum-resistant in the coming year, the team has just proposed a major upgrade that has sparked a buzz across the XRP community.

Sparking fresh excitement across the XRP community earlier today, the XRP Ledger (XRPL) Foundation has disclosed plans to introduce quantum-resistant accounts to users.

The team revealed plans to introduce a new feature to the XRP Ledger to offer users strict protection against future quantum computing threats without paying additional transaction fees.

While it is yet to be implemented, the proposed design will allow users to add a NIST-approved ML-DSA-44 signing key to their accounts and disable their existing keys.

This way, it will become harder for potential quantum attacks to compromise accounts, especially those whose public keys have already been exposed.

Apart from the extra layer of protection the upgrade promises to offer users, its cost-effective design has stirred more interest among the XRP community.

Notably, users will not have to pay higher transaction fees to access the additional security, as the proposed feature allows XRP holders to strengthen their account protection without worrying about extra transaction costs.

The proposed upgrade sends relief across the crypto ecosystem as it addresses concerns about the possibility of increasingly powerful quantum computers eventually breaking some of the cryptographic systems currently used to secure blockchains and cryptocurrencies.

While the upgrade is yet to launch, the team revealed that it has scheduled a migration study for the first half of 2027.

The study is aimed at examining how existing accounts can transition to the new security system and what the process would involve for users.

The proposed security upgrade has further sparked more optimism as it is also part of Ripple's broader aim of achieving full quantum protection by 2028.

Schwab adds Solana, Avalanche and Chainlink as crypto trading slows

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Schwab adds three crypto assets as trading activity weakens across major platforms and Wall Street seeks retail demand.

Charles Schwab added Solana, Avalanche and Chainlink to its direct crypto trading service on Nov. 9, giving customers five assets to trade just as the industry's latest disclosures show fewer active users on some platforms and smaller trades on others.

Coinbase's monthly transacting users averaged 7.4 million in the third quarter, down from 9.3 million a year earlier.

Banks and brokerages could win existing holders' business without expanding overall participation, while easier access can redistribute trading revenue even when the overall market is subdued.

Schwab's token additions follow early access in May and a rollout to all eligible retail clients in October. Its service now offers Bitcoin, Ethereum and the three newly added tokens.

Fidelity's current trading lineup includes Bitcoin, Ethereum, Fidelity Digital Dollar, Litecoin and Solana through separate Fidelity Crypto accounts. SoFi began a phased consumer rollout on Nov. 11, 2025, bringing crypto into the same app as its other financial services.

These offerings place crypto inside financial relationships customers may already have.

Coinbase disclosed its user decline in its Oct. 29 filing, attributing its year-over-year decline in monthly transacting users primarily to fewer trading users under current market conditions.

Quarterly-average MTUs: 9.3 million in Q3 2025 and 7.4 million in Q3 2026. Includes passive activity and some non-revenue transfers; this is not a count of unique investors leaving crypto.

The MTU measure includes passive activity and some non-revenue-generating transfers, and the quarterly figure averages monthly measurements.

Coinbase also warns that MTUs can overstate unique consumers. The decline measures activity on Coinbase rather than departures from crypto as a whole.

Gemini's second-quarter results put exchange trading volume at $4 billion, down from $11 billion a year earlier, while monthly transacting users increased 11% to 580,000.

More users were present, but substantially less money changed hands.

At eToro, preliminary August figures showed the invested amount per crypto trade fell to $97 from $325 a year earlier, a 70% decline. The number of crypto trades fell a much smaller 4%, to 5.3 million.

The platform's funded accounts rose 18% to 4.36 million, including 110,000 accounts from acquisitions.

Robinhood also reported third-quarter app crypto notional volume of $21 billion, down 48% year over year, and crypto transaction revenue of $120 million, down 55%.

Outside individual platforms, Gallup's June ownership survey estimated that 9% of US adults held crypto, down from 14% in 2025. Its weighted probability-based sample covered 2,043 adults.

The disclosures show softness across several businesses and in one national ownership measure, but users can hold multiple accounts, ownership differs from trading, and the reporting periods vary.

CoinGecko's third-quarter report put trading volume on the top 10 centralized spot exchanges at $1.65 trillion, down 14.2% from $1.93 trillion in the second quarter.

CoinGecko's broader average daily trading-volume measure recovered from $58 billion in July to $95 billion in September. Outstanding positions on the top 10 perpetual-futures exchanges also increased 40.6%, ending the quarter above $100 billion.

Coinbase Institutional's Nov. 2 analysis then reported that October spot volume fell 16%, while options volume rose 9%.

CoinGecko's eight-exchange liquidity report, updated Sept. 30, found Bitcoin's sampled order-book liquidity nearly 50% above its 2025 comparison, while Solana's fell more than 28.5%.

Trading activity and available market depth are moving in different directions.

In Schwab's survey released Oct. 7, six in 10 current crypto investors expected to increase their holdings over the following 12 months.

A poll by the National Cryptocurrency Association and Harris offers another indication of potential demand. In an online survey of 2,017 US adults conducted Sept. 15-16, 42% said crypto access would make them more likely to open a bank account or use it for more of their financial needs.

Even the size of the potential audience is uncertain, since Schwab reported 21% crypto ownership in its unweighted online sample of Americans ages 21-75, compared with Gallup's 9% estimate from a weighted probability-based survey of adults 18 and older.

Crypto.com's on-chain-based estimate put worldwide ownership at 774 million in June, up 4.5% from 741 million in December 2025.

A provider can see a large commercial opportunity without expecting an immediate revival in speculative turnover. The addressable holder base and the revenue generated by each active customer are separate questions.

Existing holders moving trading to a bank or brokerage would shift market share, dormant customers returning would increase activity within an existing investor base, and first-time buyers would expand participation.

Three illustrative customer routes show why a brokerage gaining business would not necessarily mean more people own crypto.

An existing holder could move trading from an exchange to a bank or brokerage. The provider gains share without necessarily adding a crypto owner.

A dormant holder could resume trading through the added access. Activity could rise within the existing investor base.

A first-time buyer could enter crypto through a familiar financial platform. This would broaden participation.

Migration shifts share, reactivation restores activity, and first-time adoption expands participation.

Coinbase reported that subscriptions and services accounted for 49% of third-quarter net revenue. Its trading market share increased and paid Coinbase One subscriptions reached a record, showing that weaker activity measures can coexist with growth elsewhere in a platform.

Evidence of a successful expansion would come from funded crypto accounts, unique active traders, assets transferred, and incremental trading volume and revenue. Disclosing whether users are new to crypto or switching providers would distinguish adoption from redistribution.

This film festival logo proves originality isn't dead in graphic design

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

KIFF's redesigned logo feels fresh and alive.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Film festivals don't have a direct impact on most people's lives. Few of us actually get around to attending one, and the films they feature rarely reach our nearest arts cinema, let alone the local multiplex. Yet, whether we ever buy a ticket to one, film festivals really do matter.

This is where new talent, both in front of and behind the camera, gets to show people what they're made of; where fresh and off-the-wall ideas get their first airing in public. A typical festival is a bubbling cauldron of visual styles, storytelling techniques and new voices, which will then gradually filter into mainstream cinema, advertising, music videos and more.

Occasionally too, a festival hit will break out in its own right. Reservoir Dogs started its journey at Sundance; Parasite at Cannes. Even if you've seen neither film, you'll have felt their ripple effects on culture in general.

Wth all that heated experimentation and ambition, you'd think a film festival logo would be another canvas for creativity. But too often, these events seem to just throw together a bunch of elements (a camera, a film reel, a clapperboard) without much thought. So it was a pleasant surprise to see the logo for the Kolkata International Film Festival (KIFF) does something very different.

Kolkata, formerly known as Calcutta, is the capital of West Bengal in India. This year the 32nd edition of its annual film festival runs from 16 to 23 December. Recently, its organisers revealed this new logo (shown above), which is the work of Sandip Ray.

Sandip is not only a filmmaker himself, he's also the son of the legendary Satyajit Ray: a director who was also a designer working on book covers, film posters and typefaces. And in this logo, Sandip has done his father proud.

Where the typical film festival logo juggles several references, this one commits to a single idea, and is all the better for it. Heavy black letters are cut into by burnt-orange triangles, each edged with sprocket holes like a strip of film.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

If someone had described that to me before I'd seen it, I'd probably have thought "That sounds awful". Film strips are the clip art of cinema: as big a cliché as a popcorn box, and increasingly anachronistic to boot.

But here, the execution is everything. Crucially, Sandip hasn't used the film strips as decoration of the logo, but as its foundation. They're not just a way of adding interest to the logo design; they are the logo design. It's the difference between committing to an idea and not actually having one in the first place.

To my mind, the triangles could be seen as doubling up as play and rewind buttons… although admittedly this might just be me.

Overall, the important thing about this logo is that it doesn’t look like anything else. I've spent so many years looking at perfectly formed sans serifs that it's made me weary. And the idea that more and more people are going to use AI tools that serve up an average of this style depresses me even more.

KIFF’s logo, in contrast, is chunky, angular and a bit odd. Some people might dislike it for that very reason. But I love the fact that two colours and one bold idea are all it needs to be instantly recognisable, and that’s got to be an inspiration for logo designers everywhere.

Tom May is an award-winning journalist specialising in art, design, photography and technology. He is the author of the books The 50 Greatest Designers (Arcturus) and Great TED Talks: Creativity (Pavilion). Zelda: Ocarina Of Time "Off To A Great Start", Says Nintendo

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Nintendo has released the Q&A translation from its latest financial briefing, with president Shuntaro Furukawa stating that Zelda: Ocarina of Time is "off to a great start".

The comment comes off the back of a question related to the decrease in hardware sales for the Switch 2, which saw a dip of 37% year-on-year. Furukawa-san explained that Japan saw a significant spike in sales prior to the price revision in May before settling down after. Overseas, meanwhile, Nintendo allowed customers ample time to purchase the Switch 2 before the price went up while also offering multiple bundles.

With the release of the Zelda 40th Anniversary console, Furukawa-san said that this "contributed to strong sales in various regions". At the time of the Q&A, he explains that while it's too early to provide specific figures for Ocarina of Time, the game is off to a great start, with Nintendo in good shape for the coming holiday season.

Nintendo usually holds off on providing specific sales figures until its quarterly financial briefings, so it's possible we might not know how well Ocarina of Time sold for a little while yet. That said, if it's performing particularly well, it's equally likely that Nintendo will provide a separate announcement via its investor relations page and social media accounts. We'll keep an eye out either way.

For the UK at least, we know that the physical release was the second-biggest in series history, beating Breath of the Wild yet falling short of Tears of the Kingdom, with the Switch's high install base likely a big factor for the latter. As for console sales, Nintendo is maintaining its current forecast of 16 million total sales for the fiscal year.

Oh, So That’s Who’s Buying All Those New Subaru Trailseeker EVs…

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

After EV sales in the US crashed into a brick wall last year, the Subaru Trailseeker has been one of the few points of light. Newly introduced this year, Trailseeker has racked up a relatively decent sales track record in the crowded SUV field. As for whether or not Subaru can continue to grow its EV sales in the US, the automaker’s elaborate, multi-faceted “Subaru Loves Pets” campaign has emerged as the secret weapon…

The Subaru Loves Pets campaign first appeared 18 years ago, back in 2008. A lot of demographic and cultural water has passed under the bridge since then. More so than in past generations, pets have taken on the role of family members equal to, if not superior to, anyone else in the household. And if that’s the case, shouldn’t pets have their own seatbelts, too?

Now Subaru’s longtime relationship with the pet-centered population is beginning to muscle into EV territory. One indication of a connection between pet and EV preferences surfaced in October, when Subaru released a new report indicating that pets wield a noticeable influence on purchasing  preferences in the US auto market overall.

Commissioned from the firm Talker Research, the report is based on interviews with 2,000 and pet owners in the US. Of that cohort, a resounding majority of 73% said they are more likely to drive for the holidays, specifically in order to travel with their pet.

“As the holidays approach, more than two-thirds (68%) said their pet will affect their travel plans this year, and nearly half (45%) have turned down travel opportunities that couldn’t accommodate their pet,” Subaru adds.

Households with pets also have distinct priorities for a vehicle. “Plenty of room between pets, passengers, and luggage tops the list of features pet owners say matter when traveling with an animal,” Suburu reports.

“More than one in four (26%) prioritize cargo space for crates, carriers, beds, or gear. Among hybrid and EV drivers, that climbs to 35%,” the automaker further notes.

If you missed the money quote, it’s that thing about 35% of hybrid and EV drivers prioritizing space for pet gear, compared to just 26% of drivers overall. In addition to the traveling-with-pets angle, that generational difference also reflects a sharp generational divergence in attitudes towards EVs.

“Electric vehicles have become a political flashpoint in Washington. But new national polling finds the biggest divide among potential EV buyers isn’t political. It’s generational,” reports the advocacy organization ZETA (the Zero Emission Transportation Association) in September.

“Seventy-five percent of adults ages 18–34 and 72% of adults ages 35–49 say they are likely to purchase or lease a new EV within the next five years,” ZETA elaborates. “Among adults ages 50–64, that number falls to 50%. Among those 65 and older, it drops to just 23%, a striking 52-point gap between the youngest and oldest Americans.”

Now let’s take another look at the Subaru survey. Talker Research found that 75% of Gen Z and 70% of millennials traveled with a pet within the past five years, compared with just 58% of Gen X and only 40% of baby boomers (looking at you, Mitt Romney).

A similar generational difference surfaced in other areas of the survey:

Younger pet owners are also more likely to say they plan travel around their pet, with 42% of Gen Z and millennials doing so compared with 27% of Gen X and boomers.

Among pet owners who haven’t yet road-tripped with their pet, 74% of Gen Z and millennials would consider it, compared with 54% of Gen X and boomers.

Subaru pulled back on its overall electrification plan for the US market after last year’s EV sales crash, but it did follow through on the new Trailseeker, offered for sale on a limited basis in January of this year. Volume deliveries to dealers began in April of this year, and in May the Trailseeker racked up 1,074 in sales. The number is vanishingly small compared to industry leader Tesla but a promising start in the challenging US market. By August, Trailseeker had a total of almost 5,000 in sales for the year to its credit, rising to 6,006 sold by the end of October.

All else being equal, recognition from Wards Auto may help keep the momentum going. On October 13, Subaru announced that the Trailseeker earned a slot in the 2026 Wards 10 Best Engines & Propulsion Systems list.

“For 2026, Wards evaluated 26 powertrains from returning winners as well as all-new or significantly updated cars, trucks, or SUVs. They were evaluated on several criteria, including power, torque, refinement, efficiency, and innovation,” Subaru notes.

“Its 375 horsepower combined with immediate electric powertrain response always satisfies. Its range estimates also hold up well in real-world driving, interstate and all. Yet Subaru keeps the price completely reasonable given such capability,” said Robin Warner, who manages the 10 Best Engines & Propulsion Systems program.

By “completely reasonable,” Warner means the Trailseeker’s starting MSRP of $39,995 and its estimated range of 281 miles. According to Subaru, the Trailseeker can go from 10% to 80% charged in as little as 28 minutes, with up to 150 kW on a DC-fast charger.

“With an onboard battery preconditioning system, enabled either by navigating to a charging destination or by the driver via touchscreen controls, the Trailseeker can charge its battery in cold climates — as low as 14 degrees Fahrenheit — at speeds comparable to those in normal weather conditions,” Subaru also advises.

In addition to the seal of approval from Wards, on October 29 Subaru announced that Trailseeker earned the “Car of the Year” award in the 2027 TopGear.com US Car Awards program

The automaker cites Top Gear US Correspondent Alex Kalogiannis, who was particularly enthused about the vehicle’s off-road performance. ““Off the beaten path, the set-and-forget X-MODE figures out all the tricky terrain stuff as you trundle through the bush, and with about 8.5 inches of ground clearance, it can scale all but the scariest obstacles in your path,” Kalogiannis remarked, somwhat colorfully.

“People still carry a torch for the WRX and its many rally-inspired incarnations, but the heart of Subaru fandom now is its dutiful, rugged, useful wagon – the Trailseeker,” he added.

That remains to be seen. Trailseeker will have to compete for the hearts and minds of Subaru fans against other models in the automaker’s lineup, including gasmobiles. The SUV-styled Outback wagon, for example, clocked 118,470 in sales from January to the end of October this year, and that was not the only Subaru model to hit six figures.

Still, US President Donald Trump has provided Subaru and other automakers with a new opportunity grow their EV sales profile this year. On February 28 the President launched his war against Iran, sending global oil prices on an upwards trajectory and improving the affordability case for EVs regardless of the loss of the $7,500 federal EV tax credit, which Trump and his Republican majority in Congress axed last fall.

Ever since dropping the first bombs on Iran, Trump has repeatedly claimed that the war is over, or almost over, or over any minute. In the latest pronouncement, on  Monday Trump said that multilateral negotiations over a peace settlement in Iran are in a state of “positive momentum toward an end.” And if you believe that…

Photo: Subaru has racked up a fairly respectable EV sales record so far this year for its new Trailseeker SUV (2026 Trailseeker cropped, courtesy of Subaru).

How to dance on a driveable car in Fortnite Treasure Hunters

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Weekly quests will have you attempting to check off all kinds of strange tasks in Fortnite. One of the weirder ones you’ll encounter in Treasure Hunters asks you to dance on a driveable car.

Quests like this one can be tough to complete, as you’re tasked with seeking out a specific item around the vast island. If you’re struggling to get this mission done, here’s how to dance on a driveable car in Fortnite.

A driveable car is any vehicle you can find and hop behind the wheel of around the Fortnite island. In Treasure Hunters, there are a few different types of cars you can find while exploring, including:

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

Drievable cars can be found all around the Fortnite island. Overall, though, they’re most commonly found sitting on roads and parked near main, named POIs. The tough part of finding these vehicles is that none of them are guaranteed to spawn in every round. Instead, they only have a chance of appearing in each match, similar to how the spawning for chests works.

There’s a chance you may encounter a driveable car while exploring around most areas on the island. Some of the overall best spots to check for them, though, are as follows.

To complete the dance on a driveable car quest in Fortnite, you need to locate a vehicle that can be driven, hop on top of it, and perform any dancing emote. As soon as you do, this quest will be complete.

For this mission, you can either simply jump on top of any driveable car, or instead actually enter a Reboot Van, switch to a roof seat, and perform a dancing emote while situated there. Both options work for this quest as long as you’re technically dancing on top of the vehicle.

There’s plenty more to discover and work on around the island after checking off this goal. Next, you might want to learn how to track how many chests you’ve opened, what all the loot odds and drop rates are, and how to get the Sweet Dreams Sprite.

A Tribute to Bunker Roy: The Man Who Turned Village Grandmothers into Solar Engineers

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Sanjit “Bunker” Roy, who taught illiterate grandmothers to become solar engineers, passed away peacefully this morning at his home in Tilonia, Rajasthan, aged 81.[1] I first encountered his work not in India, but in the rain forests of Borneo.

It was October 2016, and as night fell, the surrounding Borneo tropical rain forest had come alive with noise, dominated by the trill of frogs and the rhythmic pulse of cicadas. I was about to embark on the Sabah study tour of the Emerging Leaders Dialogue Asia (ELDA). Over the next two weeks, we would visit timber plantations, mountain communities recovering from a deadly earthquake, and marine parks.

But undoubtedly the highlight for me was discovering the inspiring story of Barefoot College, which trains village grandmothers to be solar engineers. On our first evening, while enjoying a mini feast of banana fritters, pomelo, and langsat (a bit like lychees but more delicious), we were told about the college’s remarkable partnership with the Sabah Women Entrepreneurs and Professionals Association (SWEPA) in Borneo.

Barefoot College was the brainchild of Bunker Roy, and he was an unlikely revolutionary. Born into privilege in Bengal in 1945, he was educated at the Doon School and St Stephen’s College, Delhi—two of India’s most elite institutions—and was a national squash champion. A conventional career beckoned. Then, during the Bihar famine of the mid-1960s, he saw rural poverty firsthand, and his life changed course.[2] When he told his mother he wanted to live and work in a village, he later joked, she “went into a coma” and didn’t speak to him for a long time.[3]

In 1972, Roy settled in Tilonia, a dusty village in Rajasthan, and founded the Social Work and Research Centre (SWRC), which later became known as Barefoot College. Its rules turned conventional education on its head. Anyone with a university degree was disqualified from studying there, and no degrees were awarded. In 2011, Roy said that nobody at the college earned more than $100 a month. The campus itself was built in 1986 by a dozen local “barefoot architects” who could not read or write, and it went on to win an Aga Khan Award for Architecture. At the college’s night schools, children aged 6 to 14 elect their own prime minister and cabinet.[4]

Roy’s guiding conviction was simple: “There is no urban solution to a rural problem.”[5] He was awarded the Padma Shri, one of India’s highest civilian honors, in 1986, and named one of Time magazine’s 100 most influential people in 2010.[6] He is survived by his wife, Aruna Roy, the former civil servant who led the grassroots movement behind India’s Right to Information Act.[7]

The positive impact of his work has been immense. By the college’s own count at its 50th anniversary in 2022, it had trained 1,708 illiterate and semi-literate rural women from 96 countries as solar engineers, bringing light to more than a million people in over 75,000 households and saving about 45 million liters of kerosene. Its rainwater harvesting had captured some 70 billion liters and brought safe drinking water to more than 1,650 schools. More than 90,000 working children had studied in its night schools, and its barefoot health workers had treated over 300,000 patients.[8]

During our study tour, SWEPA arranged for us to meet one of Barefoot College’s beneficiaries, a so-called solar mama. She was part of the program that takes illiterate grandmothers from poor rural villages and, within six months, trains them at the college in India for their new job. They return to their villages to install and maintain full solar lighting systems. Roy deliberately chose grandmothers because they are rooted in their communities; men and younger people, he found, were more likely to take their new skills and leave for the city.[9] Because the women shared no common language, much of the training was done through sign language and hands-on practice.[10]

Malaysia’s first solar mama—very likely the woman I met that evening—was Tarihing Masanim, from Kampung Sonsogon Magandai, near Kota Marudu in Sabah. She completed her training in India in 2015 and returned to bring electricity to around 100 homes in her village.[11] Roy loved to tell stories like hers. One of his favorites was about a 55-year-old Afghan grandmother who electrified 200 houses and set an Afghan engineering department head straight on the difference between AC and DC.[12]

The story of the solar mamas captures many elements of what it means to harness the power of responsibility to create a more inclusive society and economy. So many barriers are being broken through, as these amazing women challenge stereotypes about age, gender, and rural capacity. Barefoot College shows that sustainable development—including adopting sustainable technologies—is an option for people living with low incomes in remote villages. The Barefoot model empowers people to be agents of their own destiny.

Bunker Roy stands out as an inspiring example of inclusive leadership. His impact, which is so strongly evidenced by the solar engineers program, is as much about restoring dignity to those who are traditionally excluded as it is about developing or transferring practical skills. Roy, who said he had “a very elitist, snobbish, expensive education in India” that “almost destroyed” him, explained:

“So we started the Barefoot College, and we redefined professionalism. Who is a professional? A professional is someone who has a combination of competence, confidence and belief. A water diviner is a professional. A traditional midwife is a professional. A traditional bone setter is a professional. These are professionals all over the world. You find them in any inaccessible village around the world. And we thought that these people should come into the mainstream and show that the knowledge and skills that they have is universal.”[13]

Roy ended that talk with a line often attributed to Mahatma Gandhi, whose principles guided his life’s work: “First they ignore you, then they laugh at you, then they fight you, and then you win.”[14] For the solar mamas of Barefoot College, and for the more than a million people whose homes they have lit, Bunker Roy won.

Leading purpose-driven advisory teams, with sustainability-related Director-level roles at KPMG, Omnex, CSR International, Kaleidoscope Futures, University of Cambridge, Antwerp Management School, and Catolica Porto School of Business and Economics. Kevin De Bruyne to Everton is on, with the Manchester City legend struggling at Napoli

Everton have launched a shock bid for former Manchester City man, Kevin De Bruyne.

The Belgian is a legend of the Premier League, having won six titles and racked up an in while racking up almost 200 goal involvements in 288 appearances in the English top flight – but since leaving the Etihad, things haven't been as rosy for him in Italian football.

Now, Everton are prepared to offer the 35-year-old a return to the northwest of England in one of the biggest-name moves of the Toffees' recent history.

De Bruyne spent 10 years at Manchester City before swapping one blue shirt for another and signing for Napoli last summer.

The playmaker has struggled to make a difference in Serie A, however, with Calciomercato reporting that he “appears to be in a decline mentally and could sensationally return to the Premier League in January.”

The report notes that Everton are making a sensational swoop to bring De Bruyne back to England and have already made contact with the player's entourage.

The Merseysiders are up to sixth in the Premier League at the moment and enjoying a good season, despite leaving themselves short over the summer in terms of transfer business.

Whether club president Aurelio De Laurentiis decides to release the player midseason remains another matter.

« Elle est horrible » : beIN Sports présente ses excuses après un dérapage sexiste sur Aryna Sabalenka

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

La chaîne beIN Sports a été contrainte de présenter ses excuses officielles suite à une remarque sexiste proférée en direct visant Aryna Sabalenka. Lundi 9 novembre 2026, un commentateur a tenu des propos déplacés à l'antenne au sujet de la numéro 2 mondiale, provoquant une vive indignation. Un épisode polémique en marge du tournoi.

L'incident s'est produit le lundi 9 novembre 2026, lors de la première journée des Finales WTA disputées à Indian Wells aux États-Unis. Alors qu'un sujet diffusait des images du shooting officiel de la numéro 2 mondiale, Aryna Sabalenka, un commentateur de beIN Sports, pensant être hors antenne avant la rencontre de la Biélorusse, a lâché en direct : « Regarde, regarde, regarde. Elle est horrible ». La séquence a déclenché l'indignation de téléspectateurs sur les réseaux sociaux, certains internautes interpellant beIN Sports et l'Arcom. Une séquence qui a fait réagir.

Ce dérapage est intervenu le jour même où beIN Sports officialisait l'acquisition des droits de diffusion de l'ATP dès janvier 2028, avec neuf Masters 1000, les tournois ATP 500 et les Next Gen ATP Finals, qui viennent s'ajouter au circuit WTA et au tournoi de Wimbledon.

Interpellé sur les réseaux sociaux suite à la diffusion de ces propos, Florent Houzot, directeur de la rédaction de beIN Sports, a déclaré qu'il « regrette et condamne fermement les propos tenus ». Le dirigeant a ainsi tenu à présenter publiquement les excuses de la chaîne après cette séquence polémique. Une réaction rapide de la chaîne.

Sur le plan sportif, Aryna Sabalenka a parfaitement négocié son entrée dans le tournoi américain. La Biélorusse a remporté son premier match de poule face à Karolina Muchova en deux sets (7-6 [7/2], 7-5) en 1h53 de jeu. Une entame solide sur le court. Aryna Sabalenka disputera son deuxième match de la phase de groupes des Finales WTA le mercredi 11 novembre 2026 à Indian Wells.

Ian Hart: Champions League or a trophy for Brighton? - Why not both

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

At 62 I’ve been very lucky to have watched Brighton all over England, Wales and into Europe.

During various holidays I’ve also watched - on the TV - in the US, the Caribbean, Middle East. Last week it was the Far East and this week it was the Gold Coast, Australia at midnight. But why was Sunday’s victory so poignant for me?

Did you know with an ad-lite subscription to SussexWorld, you get 70% fewer ads while viewing the news that matters to you.

Not quite Saul on the Road to Damascus, but it’s another 90 minutes of realisation. It was perhaps prompted by sitting, as with Singapore last weekend, with ‘neutral’ football fans and hearing their opinions of ‘our’ football club.

It reiterates the almost seismic changes the club has undergone since that dramatic afternoon at Edgar Street in May 1997.

However somethings don’t change and I hope they never will, as the Albion’s annual commemoration of Remembrance Sunday was both poignant and fitting.

But then on to the action. The arrival of Brentford and the respective chairman’s shared backstory bubbling under in the background. Whilst not quite the M23 Derby perhaps, it’s a game we now look forward to that little bit extra.

Albion never let Brentford settle and by half time - courtesy of two goals and VAR actually doing it’s job - the Seagulls were effectively home and hosed.

So, if I had to don my mortar board and gown (Google required for some readers) and give a ‘quarter term’ report, what would it be?

Wow! Second in the table and the highest scorers in the top flight. One of the two defeats, Chelsea away, was arguably a game we should have won. And given Chelsea’s sporadic form since, if they played again this weekend, it certainty would be a different result.

Playing with flair and panache to reiterate a soundbite from previous columns, we all just have to enjoy the ride. One thing is for certain, we are in the EPL for at least another 12 months!

It’s just what will that 12 months bring. The much coveted trophy? Hopefully.

But in modern day football, what is the real gauge of success? We all want to see Dunky up on a podium lifting a pot, but what if this form continues for the remaining three-quarters of the campaign, and Albion get that all important Champions League spot?

Whether us old dyed in the wool fans like it or not, financially for the Albion that is far more lucrative than a trophy, bar the league title.

And again whilst trying not to get too carried away, there are also two huge factors coming at us over the hill. Champions League qualification, would almost certainly mean huge changes in the club’s wage structure.

If we don’t pay our squad the ‘going rate’, then rest assured somebody else in our league, and we all know the clubs, will. And naturally we would also have to recruit along with the financial implications that would bring.

Whoever you are, you can’t do the CL on the cheap! But again to reiterate, let’s not get carried away.

Many of us remember domestic football long before the advent of the ‘International Break’, but we go into this particular one in the highest league position we’ve ever been for a said break.

So let’s just enjoy every second of it, and cue dear old Doris Day… Que Sera, Sera…….only 28 games to go!

Rui Pinto’s lawyers in talks with UK authorities over cooperation deal for whistleblower

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

He is willing to assist in exchange for protection

Rui Pinto’s lawyers are in talks with British authorities over a potential cooperation agreement under which the Manchester City whistleblower could help to expose serious financial and sporting crime in the UK.

According to information obtained by the Guardian, the Serious Fraud Office has contacted Pinto after it emerged he was willing to assist British authorities with their investigations in exchange for protection.

William Bourdon, Pinto’s lawyer, who has represented whistleblowers such as Edward Snowden and Julian Assange, told the Guardian that contact had been established. “I can confirm that the process is under way,” he said, without providing further details.

If an agreement is reached, the Football Leaks founder could become a significant source of information in investigations into financial crime involving football, law firms and powerful business figures. Between 2015 and 2019, Pinto collected tens of millions of files containing sensitive information. He may be able to assist the SFO in identifying potential tax offences and other irregularities that would otherwise have gone undetected.

The development comes at a significant moment for the 38-year-old Pinto, with his future under Portugal’s witness protection programme uncertain.

Pinto rose to prominence by exposing some of the football industry’s most closely guarded secrets. These included Cristiano Ronaldo’s tax affairs, plans by Europe’s leading clubs to establish a breakaway European Super League and a series of controversial financial arrangements. His revelations triggered investigations and contributed to legal proceedings involving clubs and players across several countries.

More recently, documents collected and leaked by the whistleblower became relevant to the Premier League’s investigation into Manchester City, which resulted in the club being found in breach of financial rules.

The information gathered by Pinto is in the possession of French authorities, who sought previously to include him in a witness protection programme. His arrest by Portuguese police in Hungary in January 2019 disrupted those plans, although France has maintained contact with him. Between his arrest in Hungary and his extradition to Portugal, French authorities travelled to Budapest and copied the contents of the electronic devices in his possession.

Pinto was informed on 30 September, just as his name had made headlines again after the City ruling, that he would no longer be protected by the Portuguese authorities after six years under the witness protection programme. Although the authorities maintained there was no threat to his life, he was advised not to express opinions on controversial issues, to avoid crowded places and to limit the time spent with family members.

The best of our sports journalism from the past seven days and a heads-up on the weekend’s action

Pinto was given 10 days’ warning, even though a decision had been taken in July, and the ruling’s implementation was suspended after it was established that he had not been given an opportunity to mount a challenge or appeal. He then appealed and is awaiting a final ruling.

In 2023 Pinto was given a suspended four-year prison sentence in Portugal for attempted extortion and cybercrime offences. He was acquitted in a second case involving similar charges two years later. The public prosecutor’s office and Benfica, one of the clubs hacked by Pinto, appealed against the ruling. A decision from the higher court is awaited.

Supporters regard Pinto as a whistleblower who cast light on corruption, whereas detractors argue that his tactics breached legal standards and moral principles. His disclosures reached far beyond football. Details involving magistrates, legal professionals and public officials were made public and he served as the whistleblower behind Luanda Leaks, which exposed the inner workings of the business empire of Africa’s richest woman, Isabel dos Santos. She denied corruption allegations.

The Most Expensive Artworks by Pablo Picasso Ever Sold at Auction

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Over the course of seven decades, Pablo Picasso maintained prolific periods of artistic output that cemented his status as the one of most coveted artists of all time. The modernist’s most prized paintings derive from various stages in his life: the early-career Rose and Blue periods and his groundbreaking experiments in Cubism, through his output in the years leading up to and during World War II up to the late 1960s, when he was already world famous.

Central to Picasso’s oeuvre are portraits of his various muses: Marie-Thérèse Walter, Dora Maar, Françoise Gilot, and Jacqueline Roque, each of whom the artist had a series of complicated affairs. These portraits, which range from sensual to tormented, have become some of the most recognizable in art history.

In the years since his death in 1973, Picasso’s stature as an artist elevated to the highest ranks, with some of his works going for more than $100 million in public auctions and reportedly at even higher sums in private deals. Currently, he remains the top-grossing artist at auction worldwide, raking in $245 million across 3,400 lots in 2020 alone. “He is in many ways the gold standard of the art market,” said dealer Larry Gagosian in an interview with ARTnews. “I think he’ll remain that way for the foreseeable future.” Below, a look at the top auction sales of works by the Spanish master.

Femme assise depicts French artist and model Fernande Olivier, who went with Picasso to a remote village in Spain during the summer of 1909. While there, she posed for a number of paintings. One of the few early Picasso Cubist works still in private hands, Femme assise sold for £43.2 million ($63.4 million) at a Sotheby’s London evening sale in 2016, where it exceeded the $43.1 million presale estimate. A telephone bidder won the painting, which had remained under the same roof for four decades. The anonymous seller had bought it in 1973 at Sotheby’s for £340,000 ($486,000). With many of Picasso’s early Cubist works in museum collections, Femme assise was a rarity on the open market at the time. “There hasn’t been a painting of this importance at auction in potentially a generation,” Helena Newman, Sotheby’s head of Impressionist and modern art in London, said at the time.

This vibrant red portrait of Dora Maar measures only 25⅝ by 21¼ inches. Auctioned by Christie’s New York in May 2015 at the house’s “Looking Forward to the Past” sale, it went for $67.4 million to an anonymous buyer, surpassing its $55 million high estimate.

Consigned by Las Vegas casino mogul and top collector Steve Wynn, the work was the third-highest sale of the night, which also saw the record-setting sale of Picasso’s Delacroix-inspired Les femmes d’Alger Version “O” for $179.4 million (see below). Before Buste de femme was acquired by Wynn, it had passed through the hands of the artist’s granddaughter Marina Picasso and Geneva modern art dealer Jan Krugier.

Bearing a second painting on the reverse, the rare double-sided canvas La gommeuse was painted by Picasso when he was only 19 years old. The artist created the picture while grieving the death of his friend and fellow artist Carles Casagemas, who had recently died by suicide—a tragedy that ushered in the Blue Period.

The work sold for $67.45 million (including buyer’s premium) at Sotheby’s New York in November 2015, though it was hammered in below its presale estimate of $60 million. The painting’s seller, American billionaire Bill Koch, had purchased it for $3 million in 1984 at auction. It wasn’t until 2000, while the work was being conserved, that a hidden portrait mocking Picasso’s friend and patron Pere Mañach was discovered on the reverse.

Before Koch owned it, La gommeuse had passed through the hands of esteemed French art dealer Ambroise Vollard, New York dealer Lucien Demotte, and Austrian-American film director Josef von Sternberg, who bought it in 1935 and sold it in 1949 at Sotheby’s for $3,600.

Picasso created his famed Guernica and The Weeping Woman in 1937 amid the tumultuous years of the Spanish Civil War. Painted the same year, Femme au béret et à la robe quadrillée features a more joyous image, that of his muse Marie-Thérèse Walter in a blue, red, and white outfit set against a purple background.

The painting made its auction debut during a Sotheby’s Impressionist and modern art sale in London. At that February 2018 sale, the work fetched £49.8 million (approximately $68.7 million); the winning bidder was Harry Smith, the London-based chairman of the art advisory firm Gurr Johns, who purchased it along with three other Picassos from the sale. It had changed hands only once before that auction: After Picasso’s death, his estate sold it to an unknown private collector.

Picasso’s Femme nue couchée depicts his muse Marie-Thérèse Walter reclining in a state of sensual abandon. The fluid brushwork, vivid contrasts of color, and radically distorted biomorphic forms lend the female body an almost sculptural presence. The Château de Boisgeloup in Normandy, where the painting was created, provided a studio for Picasso’s experiments with form and three-dimensional work.

The painting remained in Picasso’s collection until his death, then with his heirs until 2008, when it entered a private collection. Offered publicly for the first time, it headlined Sotheby’s Modern Evening Auction in New York on May 17, 2022. With an estimate reported at approximately $60 million, it achieved $67.5 million including fees; the hammer price was $58.5 million. The result made it the evening’s top lot.

Hidden from the public for almost 40 years, Dora Maar au chat portrays Picasso’s muse seated in a chair with a black cat on her shoulder. The painting was first acquired by Paris dealer Pierre Colle in 1946 and was later owned by Chicago collectors Leigh and Mary Block.

In 2006, Chicago’s Gidwitz family decided to auction the painting at Sotheby’s New York, where it sold for $95.2 million, almost twice the $50 million low estimate. Sotheby’s granted the family a $53 million guarantee in order to secure the painting, a rarity at the time.

At the evening sale, five bidders competed for the work. Reportedly among them were casino magnate Steve Wynn, Ohio retailer Leslie Wexner, and Microsoft cofounder Paul Allen. But the portrait of Maar went instead to an eager bidder in the room wielding a paddle with the number 1340, in an unusually visible move for a transaction of that level at a marquee auction.

The sale drew speculation as to the identity of the winning bidder, with many suspecting that he was an ultrawealthy collector from Russia. Ultimately, it was revealed that the anonymous floor bidder bought the work for the Georgian mining tycoon Bidzina Ivanishvili. New to the art world at the time, Ivanisvhili, who would go on to serve as Georgia’s prime minister, has a modern and contemporary art collection worth $1 billion, most of which he keeps in storage in London, with replicas of his masterpieces adorning his Tbilisi residence.

Leading Christie’s 20th-century art evening sale in New York in May 2020, Picasso’s Femme assise près d’une fenêtre (Marie-Thérèse) from 1932 sold for $103.4 million, almost double the $55 million pre-sale estimate. After a 19-minute long spar between six bidders, the painting finally sold to a bidder on the phone with Vanessa Fusco, Christie’s co-head of the 20th-century art evening sale, in a moment that proved the art market had returned in full force after a period of economic lag caused by the coronavirus pandemic.

Before its sale at Christie’s, it was last exhibited at Paris’s Picasso Museum showcase “Picasso 1932, année érotique” in 2017, alongside mega-collector Steven Cohen’s 1932 Picasso portrait Le Rêve, which he purchased for $155 million in 2013. The following year, Femme assis went to the Tate Modern in London for an exhibition titled “The EY Exhibition: Picasso 1932, Love Fame Tragedy.”

The work is notable both for its large size, and in its unusual depiction of Picasso’s muse, Marie-Thérèse Walter, who is often portrayed as sleeping or dreaming. Here, she is sitting upright in a chair, as if enthroned with her gaze directed at the viewer. The most striking aspect of the piece, Fusco told ARTnews ahead of the sale, is “the incredible presence of this woman and the agency which he’s given her, which you don’t always see in his depictions of her.”

Picasso’s painting of a seated Parisian teenager smoking a pipe and wearing a crown of roses is a signature work from the artist’s Rose Period and it is one of the works from this era that remains in private hands.

In May 2004, the work was offered at an evening sale in New York by Sotheby’s, alongside 33 other pieces from its owners Mr. and Mrs. John Hay Whitney to raise funds for the Greentree Foundation, the family’s charitable organization. The anonymous bidder, who purchased it for $104.1 million (well above the $70 million pre-sale estimate), beat out six others who were vying for it that evening.

The Whitneys had purchased it from a Zurich-based dealer for around $30,000 in 1950. The buyer is believed to have been Guido Barilla, the billionaire heir and chairman of the Barilla pasta company. When it sold in 2004, Garçon à la pipe became the most expensive ever sold at auction, beating out Van Gogh’s Portrait of Dr. Gachet (1890), which had sold for $82.5 million in 1990. Garçon à la pipe held that record until May 2010.

In May 2010, the star lot of an evening sale of Impressionist and modern art at Christie’s New York was Picasso’s Nude, Green Leaves and Bust, from his 1932 series of paintings of Marie-Thérèse Walter. During the sale, eight bidders competed for the work, driving the hammer well past the $70 million expectation. It ultimately sold for $106.5 million to a phone bidder.

Held in private hands for 60 years, the painting was sold by Los Angeles philanthropist Frances Lasker Brody, who had purchased it with her husband, Sidney F. Brody, from New York dealer Paul Rosenberg in January 1951 for $17,000.

The sale of Peggy and David Rockefeller’s art collection at Christie’s New York, which included top lots by Paul Gauguin, Edouard Manet, Henri Matisse, and Claude Monet, set a historic high for a single-owner sale in 2018. The piece that fetched the highest price was Picasso’s portrait Fillette a la corbeille fleurie, made during the artist’s Rose Period.

The work’s first owners were Leo and Gertrude Stein, the American siblings and early patrons of modern art, who bought it from a Paris dealer the year it was completed. After Gertrude Stein’s death, the painting was passed to her companion, Alice B. Toklas, whose estate sold it in 1968 to a syndicate of collectors associated with the Museum of Modern Art in New York. David Rockefeller then purchased it for himself.

When the painting sold for a staggering $115 million during the blockbuster Rockefeller auction in 2018, its price wasn’t the only reason the piece attracted attention. Featuring a nude prepubescent girl, wearing only a necklace and holding a basket of red flowers, the work generated controversy over Picasso’s sexualized image of a child, known among historians to be an underage flower seller and sex worker named Linda.

The work has long been considered a masterpiece, though. “She represents the themes that Picasso would wrestle with for his life—love, sex, beauty, tenderness, violence,” said Marc Porter, chairman of Christie’s Americas. Since the record sale of the work, criticism among scholars surrounding Picasso’s treatment of women has persisted. In 2021, protesters staged a demonstration outside Barcelona’s Picasso Museum in an attempt to bring attention to the issue.

In La Femme à la montre, Marie-Thérèse Walter appears elegantly dressed, wearing a wristwatch and sitting in an armchair against a blue background. The painting features bright colors, geometric shapes, and a harmonious composition. Her affair with Picasso remained secret for years because the artist was married to Olga Khokhlova. The wristwatch may symbolize the passage of time and the complexity of their romance.

In 1966, collector Ernst Beyeler purchased the painting directly from Picasso’s studio in Mougins, France. Two years later, Emily Fisher Landau acquired it. It remained a centerpiece of her collection for nearly 50 years, displayed above the fireplace in her Manhattan residence until her death in 2023. On November 8 of that year, Sotheby’s auctioned the painting in New York for $139.4 million, making it Picasso’s second-most expensive artwork sold at auction, after Les Femmes d’Alger (Version “O”).

In 1956, the legendary Picasso collector Victor Ganz put in an offer to buy all 15 pieces in Picasso’s Delacroix-inspired “Les Femmes d’Alger” series (1954–55) from Paris dealer Henry Kahnweiler for $212,500. In years to follow, Ganz kept just five from the group after selling the rest of them to recoup his purchase price; Version “O” was among the few he held on to.

Decades later, in 1997, Christie’s staged the blockbuster sale of Ganz’s prized Picasso collection in New York, where the piece went for $31.9 million to London dealer Libby Howie, who is said to have purchased it on behalf of a buyer in Europe. Nearly two decades later, at Christie’s New York in May 2015, Version “O” resurfaced on the market and set a record price of $179.4 million, making it the most expensive work to sell at auction at the time. Reported to have been purchased by the Qatari royal Al Thani family, that painting remains the most expensive Picasso ever to be auctioned.

Primary Architects crowns carpenter's studio in rural China with undulating timber roof

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Chinese studio Primary Architects has topped a carpenter’s studio in China with a sinuous timber roof that responds to the local mountain terrain.

Located at the entrance of Haotang Village, Xinyang, in China’s central Henan Province, the building belongs to Zhang, a local carpenter.

It was designed by Primary Architects to incorporate his studio, as well as a woodcraft workshop that is also open to the public.

Primary Architects conceived of the project, called the Carpenter’s Home, as a lightweight timber structure nestled between the mountain and the village and aligned with the natural elevation of the terrain.

The 405-square-metre building sits at the foot of the mountain and is topped by a roof made up of hyperbolic curved beams that span up to 26 metres.

This sloping roof appears to gently descend from the mountainside and reconciles the elevation difference between the mountain and the village.

“The building responds to the rolling mountain terrain naturally by treating its roof as a gentle extension of the mountain ridge stretching into the village,” Primary Architects explained.

According to the studio, its undulating outline not only echoes the surrounding mountain terrain, but also forms a structural system that enables a flexible arrangement of internal spaces.

The roof’s structural system, which is comprised of glued timber and steel joints, was created through parametric control and digital modelling.

Prefabricated in a factory, the modular roof was assembled on site to improve construction efficiency.

Inside Carpenter’s Home, the ground floor houses the carpenter’s own woodworking shop as well as an area that is open to tourists and visitors, where exhibitions and tours are held.

A mezzanine floor has a tea room for visitors. Like the rest of the space, it is lit by a series of curved skylights that were incorporated into the timber-structured roof to introduce natural light into the interiors.

The angle of each skylight was carefully positioned in accordance with the movement of the sun to allow the interiors of the studio to be well-lit during working hours.

The open interiors were designed both to accommodate the traditional wood-working practice and to function as an education centre for the craft.

An expansive staircase that doubles as seating connects the studio space on the ground floor to the mezzanine tea room.

From here, visitors can observe Zhang’s working process, as well as his tools and material samples displayed on the side walls.

Primary Architects also created a series of working benches that can be combined and divided to support various aspects of the carpenter’s work.

“The Carpenter’s Home seeks to break through the existing formal paradigms of rural construction, responding to the land, craftsmanship and daily life in a more imaginative way,” said Primary Architects.

“With contemporary timber structure as its medium, it brings traditional woodworking back to rural daily life, making the building not only a space for accommodating activities, but also a continuously unfolding lifestyle,” it continued.

“For Haotang Village, it is not only a craft workshop, but also a timber vessel embodying local memory, artisan spirit and the vision of the future countryside.”

Primary Architects is a Beijing-based architecture studio founded by Xiang Huang in 2020. This project was shortlisted for workplace project (small) category at this year’s Dezeen Awards.

Elsewhere in China, Büro Ziyu Zhuang designed a museum cluster to “blend seamlessly” into a meadow, while RSHP completed a skyscraper in Shenzhen with a “striking butterfly layout”.

Chief architects: Xiang Huang, Songyan Mao Architectural design team: Cong Liu, Zhiyan Lin, Xiaoxia Wang, Junjie Liu, Xiaofeng Zhang, Yao Lu Grade A design institute: Zhongke Architectural Planning and Design Research Institute                                                                         Wood structure detailed design: Bowen Li Construction company: Shanxi Junchen Construction Co., Ltd. Client: Xinyang Pingqiao District Juxing Agricultural Investment and Development Co., Ltd. Building area: 405 square meters

"Why Is It Not Fun?" - Doug Bowser Gives A Peek Into Nintendo's Strategy Sessions

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

It's no secret that when it comes to game development, Nintendo is all about fun. We all know this firsthand, of course, but it's still interesting to get a peek behind the curtain and find out how this mission statement works its way into development itself.

At the recent GamesBeat Next 2026 event, former Nintendo of America President Doug Bowser provided exactly that. Joining fellow former industry higher-ups Peter Moore and Shawn Layden on the 'Game Legends' panel, Bowser discussed how Nintendo's ultimate objective, "We make smiles", carries through into game development and internal reviews.

Bowser recalls sitting in on a strategy session with legendary Nintendo producer Shinya Takahashi, in which the dev wasn't interested in how the testers were playing the game, but rather how the game was affecting the testers. "He wanted enough people in the room that you could see their faces and see their eyes, but he didn’t want any more than that," Bowser said. "He would sit there and literally watch your expression. He never watched what your hands were doing on a controller. He never watched what was happening on the screen. He watched your eyes and your facial expression."

This would continue into session feedback. "He wanted to see, are you having fun?," Bowser continued. "If he didn’t see it, he would ask you directly. 'Why is it not fun?' That was direct feedback that you got."

It all sounds like a pretty strenuous ordeal (there's no magic dev button to 'add fun', after all), but hey, it clearly works for Nintendo — the house behind some of the funnest games in history.

Even though Bowser is no longer heading up NoA, having been replaced by Devon Pritchard earlier this year, he said to the panel that this smile-first approach is still important today, particularly considering new generations' needs "are fundamentally different than ours are."

Pointing to the likes of Fortnite, Roblox and Minecraft, Bowser argued that younger gamers "do want fun, and they want it to be easy to get to fun," but it's increasingly about self-expression in gaming. "It’s important for us to keep an eye on those cohorts as we build our next games."

Elsewhere in the panel, the former game leaders discussed AI in game development (something they all seem rather positive on), the console wars, exclusives and more. You'll find the full transcript on Games Beat.

Crystal Palace are reportedly weighing up a managerial change, with former Tottenham Hotspur boss Thomas Frank emerging as a leading candidate to take charge at Selhurst Park.

The Eagles have endured a disappointing start to the Premier League campaign, and the club’s hierarchy is increasingly concerned about their performances under Pierre Sage. According to reports from NY Times, Frank is among the managers Palace is considering as they assess options ahead of a potential change in the dugout.

The Danish manager could be an attractive option for Crystal Palace as they look to bring stability to the team. Frank enjoyed considerable success at Brentford, guiding the club into the Premier League and helping them establish themselves in the top flight.

His experience in English football could prove valuable for Palace, who need someone to improve their defensive organisation and get the best out of their existing squad. However, his spell at Tottenham was less successful, and he will be keen to rebuild his reputation after leaving the north London club earlier this year.

Palace have collected just seven points from their opening 10 league matches, winning twice and conceding 24 goals. Their recent 3-1 defeat against Liverpool was another setback, leaving them at the bottom of the table and increasing the pressure on Sage.

The Eagles have also struggled to maintain defensive consistency, and their inability to address set-piece problems has become a major concern. A managerial change could give the players a fresh start, although the club will need to make the right appointment.

Frank is not the only experienced manager being considered, with Sean Dyche and Vitor Pereira also mentioned as potential alternatives. Dyche, in particular, has considerable experience helping Premier League clubs navigate difficult relegation battles.

It remains to be seen whether Palace will finalise their interest in Frank, but the former Brentford manager appears to be a serious contender. A return to the Premier League could give him another chance to demonstrate his managerial qualities.

For Palace, appointing the right successor will be crucial if they want to avoid spending the rest of the campaign fighting relegation.
