Ligue 1: PSG crushes Le Havre thanks to a huge Hakimi and stays in the lead

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Paris Saint-Germain showed its power against Le Havre this Friday evening, on the occasion of the 9th day of Ligue 1. However, Luis Enrique’s men – who largely rotated his squad with Akliouche lower down and Mayulu in false 9 – were surprised from the start. At the conclusion of a superb collective action, launched by Rayan Fofana, Maja deceived Safonov with the left foot to give the Normans the advantage (1-0, 18th). The HAC even tried to take advantage of this opening score to hurt on the counter, but PSG quickly regained control of operations.

Because the Parisian reaction did not take long. Already a scorer against OL, Godts tied the two teams after a cross from Hakimi, perfectly launched into the area by Hernandez (1-1, 26th). Two minutes later, the Moroccan defender this time took on the goalscoring costume. Served in depth by Mayulu, he resisted the return of Touba before deceiving Bodart and giving his team the advantage. Despite a few situations in Le Havre, notably a dangerous strike from Nakamura repelled by Safonov, PSG returned to the locker room with an advantage.

Upon returning from the locker room, the Parisians quickly increased their lead. In the 55th minute, Hakimi scored twice with a superb free kick on the edge of the area, curled with his right foot (3-1, 55th). The Moroccan then narrowly missed the hat-trick, before finally winning it after an error from Bodart’s thigh (5-1, 69th). An exceptional evening for the Parisian full-back.

In the meantime, Mayulu took advantage of Bodart’s completely missed exit to finish in the empty goal and take off. After falling behind, Luis Enrique’s men had completely turned the match around and kept the lead in Ligue 1 after this sixth victory in a row and before meeting Villarreal on the next day of the Champions League. On the other hand, things are seriously starting to get tense for Le Havre, still bottom of the standings without the slightest victory. There is urgency before facing Lorient.

built99 guide celebrates and archives architectural intelligence of 99 indian practices

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

the built99 guide documents, celebrates, and preserves architectural intelligence from india, revealing how these practices -- and architects -- are progressing the future of our built environment.

What if the architecture in India could lead the world in sustainable, deeply human design? A comprehensive new book highlights this possibility. The inaugural Built99 guide and its companion digital directly, below, explores the future of the built environment by documenting 99 carefully selected Indian architecture and design practices. Rather than simply cataloging past achievements, this expansive volume experiments with an ongoing global dialogue, translating decades of regional research, material ethics, and civic impact into a permanent print edition. The guide examines the foundational, forward-thinking ideas driving each studio’s portfolio.

By elevating these 99 practices onto an international stage on both designboom and ArchDaily, the Built99 guide documents, celebrates, and preserves architectural intelligence from India and the Global South. As the reader turns its pages, the book (see more) reveals how these practices — and architects — are progressing the future of our built environment.

City: Chennai Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Madras Mise en Scène, Chennai, by 1405 Design Studio, rebuilds a multi-generational Rajasthani family’s 10,000 sq. ft. home on its own former footprint. The studio‘s design draws light and privacy through a tight row house plan that moves from communal ground floors to personal spaces above. A Jodhpur Haveli door, Pichwai and Madhubani works, and brass detailing weave Indian spirit without cliché, while book-matched green quartz rises through the double-height living room. The staircase, with an installation-like prayer bead, pauses inhabitants and guests alike as stage for generations to gather.

City: Punjab Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Residence Ondra, Amritsar, by 23DC Architects, unfolds as a calm, sun-aligned L-shaped home where courtyards, vertical louvres and glazing balance daylight with comfort. The architects designed a layered arrival sequence — covered parking court, waterbody, pathways and sculptural canopy — that leads toward the foyer, with the pooja room poised above water. At its heart, the double-height ‘Crystal Volume’, a soaring glazed living space anchored by a statement chandelier, connects interiors to landscape, while formal living, dining and kitchen zones flow through sliding openings to the lawn — composed, luminous, and deeply rooted in place.

City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

APL Apollo Corporate Office, Noida, by 42MM Architecture, pairs a bold exoskeletal glass facade with tiered massing that recedes into verdant terraces. A double-height atrium lobby, curved stone and open-plan floors set a refined tone by the studio, flooding interiors with light and collaborative transparency. The executive floor’s triple-height lobby, Sicis flooring and suspended glass meeting room elevate the experience further, while private suites blend classical richness with contemporary polish, which, together, define a new benchmark in luxury corporate design.

City: Kolkata Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

At the Edge of the Thar, Nokha, by Ajay Arya of A Square Designs, sets a Scandinavian-inspired holiday home within Rajasthan’s arid landscape. Local stone, wood and exposed concrete meet a charcoal-and-walnut palette, while expansive glazing frames the desert through a dramatic 24-foot living space anchored by a sculptural indoor tree. The architects positions six bedrooms and a gallery-like circulation to lend individual character, extending outdoors via a plunge pool. Quietly luxurious, the home balances Scandinavian restraint with Rajasthan’s raw, timeless spirit.

City: Kolkata Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Unscripted, a temporary pavilion at Mumbai’s Architecture & Design Film Festival, curated by Aric Chen alongside an international jury, responds to the city’s dense, restless simultaneity through porosity and interruption rather than resolution. Its structure, created by the design studio, begins as a disciplined grid before fracturing under a 48° shift, carving diagonals that unsettle straight-lined order. Voids cut into the form become thresholds framing light and encounter, where performer and audience exchange roles. Mirroring Mumbai’s own logic, the pavilion finds meaning not in walls, but in the spaces it leaves open.

City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Airyland Estate, Mussoorie, revives an early-1900s colonial home into a seven-bedroom holiday retreat steeped in Raj-era charm. Restored facades, limestone walls and timber beams merge with reclaimed doors, repolished furniture and local materials, favouring intimate, inviting warmth over minimal modernity while honouring the hill station’s historic character. Overgrown gardens were revitalized with native planting, pathways and mountain-facing seating, blending old and new. The studio’s project stands as a sustainable, sensitive dialogue between heritage and contemporary living.

City: Mumbai Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Between Ground and Sky, Malappuram, by ADND is conceived as an emotional enclosure where floors ground, walls hold and roofs hover, letting wind, light and monsoon air move freely through. Laterite stone and terracotta-rendered surfaces anchor the house in Kerala’s geology, softened by earthy warmth and texture. The architects designed the spaces to flow as emotions rather than rooms — courtyards releasing like exhalations, staircases rising ethereally. Light becomes material, marking time, until architecture dissolves into stillness, a quiet sanctuary rooted in earth and shaped by sky.

City: Pune Connections: Instagram | LinkedIn

Three Portal House, Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar, by Alkesh gangwal & Associates unfolds as framed volumes and courtyards on a compact urban plot. A sculptural stone facade with deep portals and patterned apertures filters daylight into shifting shadows, while warm stone, veneer and muted fabrics ground interiors in tactile calm. Double-height volumes, skylights and pendant lights lend dramatic verticality, as courtyards, terraces and pergolas draw greenery deep inward. The house, created by the practice, balances solidity with openness, achieving privacy and light within its dense context.

City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn

The Willow Orchard House, Ladakh, by Alva Architects is built entirely in timber, site soil and hand-quarried granite, without steel or cement, withstanding extremes from +30°C to −25°C. A frost-protected shallow foundation lets the structures nestle gently into the slope among century-old willows. Sun-dried mud blocks, exposed SPF framing and traditional Markalak plaster shape the walls, while solar-oriented planning and heavy insulation retain warmth. Left structurally exposed throughout, the architects‘ building is warm, tactile and quietly engineered.

City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Abode Jaipur, reimagines a 300-year-old haveli in the UNESCO-listed Walled City as a boutique hotel with heritage soul — a sensorial retreat of cool stone, breathing lime plaster and light-tempering shutters. Built on Jaipur’s ‘nothing new, everything local’ ethos, regional craft and materials ground the project in authenticity and sustainability. Guided by careful study of traditional haveli life, the architects‘ design distils Jaipur’s chromatic richness — soft pinks, deep greens, warm marbles — into calm, rhythmic spaces of compression and release, shaped through close dialogue with local artisans.

City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Hyatt Regency, Dehradun, by And Studio stands as a landmark hospitality project in Uttarakhand’s foothills, spanning 48,000 square metres across 268 keys. Contextually intelligent in design, the studio‘s design responds directly to the evolving demands of the modern traveller through contemporary Indian architecture and conscious luxury. Blending Hyatt’s global elegance with the rich, mountainous landscape, the hotel emerges as a destination rooted in individual experience, modern in expression, yet deeply attuned to its surrounding terrain and spirit of place.

City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Centre Court Club Mocha, Gurugram, by Archi Hives is more than a building; it is an experience shaped by stillness, proportion, and material honesty. It demonstrates that true luxury lies in restraint and that classical inspiration can be reinterpreted without imitation. In a rapidly transforming urban landscape, the club offers something rare: composure. It stands as a place where architecture does not compete with life, but supports it. Through stone, symmetry, and subtlety, the practice has crafted a space that resonates beyond aesthetics. It is a sanctuary of quiet luxury — timeless, grounded, and deeply intentional.

City: Mumbai Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

BITSOM, Kalyan, Mumbai Metropolitan Region, by Architect Hafeez Contractor, spans 60 acres and 1.5 million sq. ft. as a contemporary gurukul for 5,000 students. The contractor positions a central axis to organize academic, student and faculty zones around a pedestrian first plan. Inward looking academic blocks frame a central plaza with shaded seating and dining, connected by a stilted volume linking the Centre of Excellence to the sports fields beyond, an integrated, climate responsive campus balancing institutional scale with community.

City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn

Abhiprakash Sadan, by Archna Agarwal Studio is set on a 1,100 sq. yd. plot, reinterpreting the farmhouse spirit through architecture inseparable from landscape. The studio’s uses broad glass facades, verandas and courtyards to dissolve boundaries between inside and outside, while double-height volumes lend quiet grandeur without losing intimacy. Natural stone, exposed brick and wood ground the home in warmth. At its heart, a luminous atrium anchors the flow of life, extending into gardens through bespoke doors and crafted details. More sanctuary than structure, the house doesn’t sit on the land — it belongs to it, refined by modern finishes that enhance its rustic character and create a warm, contemporary retreat.

City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Bharat Mandapam, at the edge of Lutyens’ Delhi, by Arcop Associates reinterprets the center as an icon of New India. Designed by the practice in association with Aedas, architect Sanjay Singh and Simon Griffith shaped its elliptical, fluid form inspired by the Yamuna, reflecting aspirations while acknowledging site history. Raised on an undulating landscaped podium inspired by Rashtrapati Bhawan, it creates a ‘window to Delhi’ towards Kartavya Path and India Gate. Sandstone is reinterpreted through large stone and GFRC facades juxtaposed with glass overhangs. Art and heritage integrate through installations inspired by yoga, India’s IT journey, zero and Mangalyaan.

City: Mumbai Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Ganga | Hyatt Regency, GIFT City, Gandhinagar, by ARK Reza Kabul Architects, is a mixed-use development bringing together hospitality, commercial and retail spaces across 1,600,000 sq. ft. Its fluid geometry and central structural void create a distinctive skyline identity while drawing natural light deep into the tower’s core. The architects use a two-storey retail podium to activate the street, rising past a column-free 20,000 sq. ft. ballroom into 12 hotel floors and 25 office levels, crowned by a sky realm featuring an infinity pool, gardens and panoramic dining.

Frida, a 12,000 sq. ft. Kolkata triplex by Arredatore Design Studio, embraces Nordic-inspired slow living across three floors linked by a landscaped passage, its double-height living space anchored by a sculpted concrete staircase and book-matched marble wall. Alabastrine white and taupe unify bedrooms, dens and a master suite opening onto a private terrace, while the penthouse’s 3,000 sq. ft. garden with glass house and fountain crowns the home. Prometheus School by Arvind Vivek & Associates embodies ‘Dynamic Space + Play,’ forging fluid, multi level learning environments through a networked spine of shaded courts and elevated verandas. House of Waves, Rustomjee Crown, Mumbai, by Asummi R Design, restructures a four-bedroom apartment into three bedrooms with a den. The den’s orange mandir sparks contrast against the calm, while a monochromatic master bedroom and its sage green marble bath extend the tranquillity. Atrey Design Studio, by Principal Architect Arun Sharma, translates a tree’s form into architecture — its trunk-like central columns supporting cantilevered ‘branch’ slabs within a tilted, wind-swayed silhouette on a New Delhi plot. Rafters enclose the shell while green walls, courtyards and radiant slab cooling bring environmental comfort across four storeys. The architects created a sculptural entrance, with a dried-tree installation and cascading water, that leads to open studios, a seminar floor, and a bamboo-lined VR space — each level layering raw concrete with nature’s calm. The House of Belonging, is built not around a plan, but around a family—envisioned for generations to live together, believing that belonging needs not one shared space, but freedom within connection. Harrow International School, Bengaluru, by C.P. Kukreja Architects, marks the UK institution’s first Indian campus. City: Delhi Connections: Website | Instagram | LinkedIn | Facebook

Plus, is a mixed-use building in Panchkula’s industrial belt, designed for a pharmaceutical company, integrating storage, logistics, offices, and rental retail within one cohesive form. Corner entries, conceived as glazed subtracted volumes by the practice, extend vertically into terrace gardens—brick-walled on the first floor, open-skied above—forming a sculptural, three-dimensional cross evoking the pharmaceutical symbol. Cityspace’82 Architects Studio, DLF Phase 2, Gurugram, balances openness and warmth through clean lines and a restrained palette. As a climatic filter, they moderate sunlight and heat gain, ensure privacy, and admit daylight and views, becoming a responsive interface.

William Saliba earns Arsenal U21s praise as defender continues comeback from injury

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

William Saliba Arsenal return: William Saliba has made a great impression during his return to action, according to Arsenal’s coaching staff. The U21s manager, David Horseman, praising his attitude and professionalism.

The 25-year-old played the first 45 minutes of Arsenal U21’s 5-2 win at Boreham Wood against Aston Villa on Sunday. Slowly making his way back from his three-and-a-half-month layoff.

Saliba was clearly a quality player, but it was his attitude which impressed Horseman. He worked well with the younger players and was a great example to follow throughout the morning.

The defender had been scheduled to play, and there were no new injury concerns behind his half-time withdrawal. He will return to the first team group as he continues to build up match fitness.

Saliba was praised by Horseman for his positive attitude since joining the club. His preparation, involvement in team meetings and ability to communicate with the younger players stood out.

He also showed his quality with the ball. His passing range helped the Arsenal attack and he made a good weighted pass that led to Marcell Washington running into space before Andre Harriman-Annous’ first goal.

Saliba didn’t see it as below his level to play in the game and really got into helping the academy players.

It set an example for the younger players in the squad. With an established first teamer keeping up the standards, there wasn’t much of an excuse for anyone else not to.

Saliba was not the only senior player to feature, with Marli Salmon as captain and Max Dowman also playing. Dowman, Maalik Hashi and Evan Mooney all scored, and Harriman-Annous added two goals.

It was another key step in Saliba’s return, as he featured in the matchday squad for Arsenal’s game against Slavia Prague after playing 45 minutes in the behind-closed-doors friendly.

He wasn’t selected in the senior squad for their game against Hull City. With only nine subs allowed in the Premier League, Arsenal decided it was best to play him for the U21s.

The decision means that Saliba has been able to play competitive minutes to build up his fitness.

Arsenal will be hoping that he can now make a full recovery from his injury so that Mikel Arteta has another defensive option heading into a busy period for the club.

However, the U21s will have to maintain their high standards from the Aston Villa game without first team players such as Saliba, Salmon and Dowman.

We’ll keep you updated as and when we hear more information about the William Saliba Arsenal return.

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Big Walk developer House House reveal genAI use in final stretch of development, only revealing it now as "it has become a matter of public interest"

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Friendslop du jour Big Walk is the latest in an unfortunately long series of games that have been confirmed to use generative AI in their production, with developers House House clarifying their use of it in a statement to Aftermath (thanks!). House House state that "LLMs were used sparingly by House House to help work through difficult technical challenges and bugs", and that "well under 0.1% of Big Walk’s code had anything to do with this usage", but are quick to note that "generative AI was not used in any other context when making the game."

A specific example is given in the case of Unity's apparent weirdness around shadow casters in its rendering pipeline, as it's seemingly difficult to have unnecessary shadow casters behind the player culled, which resulted in the game running at half speed around sunrise and sunset. House House explain that they couldn't find a solution themselves, asked a Unity engineer for help who said it was impossible, and then decided the only answer was to ask an LLM chatbot; "after a few dead ends, it suggested a possible fix, and while we had to modify the suggestion extensively, it worked. This fix represented about 10 lines of code." While absolutely not the most pressing thing to mention here, it is very funny to me for House House to clarify that they "had to modify the suggestion extensively", almost as if this tech isn't actually helpful in giving useful answers.

So, House House touched the stove, despite how easy it is to simply not touch the stove. The thing is, this wasn't a case of being forced to by upper management, as is sometimes the case. This was a choice made entirely voluntarily, and House House are attempting to have their cake and eat it by ending their statement with the reflection that "We have some concern that voluntary disclosures like this might contribute to the normalisation of this technology, or its treatment as “inevitable”. We support the work of those who are trying to limit the harm generative AI is doing to videogames and our world, and apologise that Big Walk’s LLM usage may not meet their standards." But then, you know, that's followed up by saying that Big Walk was developed "without an internal AI usage policy in place, and we consider that lack of policy a failure. We will work to have a more comprehensive policy going forwards." So, not to worry, they learned touching the stove is bad by touching the stove, and definitely won't do it again in future. If only there were thousands of other cases informing them why touching the stove is bad.

Very relevant to this reveal is the recent statement by select jurors of the Independent Games Festival which, after much internal deliberation, came to the conclusion that those who sign the statement would decline to vote for any game that uses generative AI in any capacity, with signees including Austin Walker, Sam Barlow, Lena Reine, Brendon Chung, Aura Triolo, aurahack and more. The statement specifies that "AI-generated code is not an exception to these harms." Fellow juror Liz Ryerson confirmed that Big Walk was being considered by the IGF, and was a matter of discussion in judging as to whether its use of genAI would be acceptable.

As for House House only disclosing this now, they state it was not only because it was necessary for their IGF submission, but also that "we have not made any disclosure on Steam as the platform does not request disclosure of code or chatbot usage", and that they have spoken on it publicly to Aftermath because "it has become a matter of public interest."

If I were to be cynical, I would say that House House would prefer to be the ones breaking this news themselves rather than having the IGF reveal that they had been hiding their use up until now; it's usually easier to control PR if you release it yourself. Big Walk had already been securely in everyone's good books for its support for the BDS boycott, for example, so why ruin that clean rep with something like a genAI disclosure?

I don't know how better to close this article than with an excerpt from that very IGF statement:

"We know that change does not come at the individual level, but as independent developers, we also know firsthand the value of pushing back on what an industry has deemed "inevitable." As jurists, we feel a responsibility to push past defeatism and aspire to a higher ideal.

The future is not yet settled. You are not alone. We can stand together."

Success story means West Ham flop could stay away for ever – Report

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

If ever there was a story detailing the importance of attitude over ability, then this tale of overcoming adversity – in the end –  involving one of West Ham’s worst transfers of the Steidten era – is certainly that.

Having vowed never to write another Niclas Fullkrug story I find myself drawn yet again to his remarkable mental resilience and fortitude, re-launching his career at 34 years old ‘back home’ in Germany after failing so conclusively at both West Ham and AC Milan – just in case anyone wants to point the finger at a Hammers’ coaching failure being solely to blame for his misfiring.

This is a player who lost his international squad spot, couldn’t stay fit for love nor money (although he had plenty of that) and eventually after a very public, failed loan in Italy, rediscovered himself and his form back in his Bundesliga home.

Life in the old dog yet at 34 – Niclas Fullkrug

Fullkrug is now the German top league’s top scorer. Just reflect on that for a second.

The guy who scored three times in two years as a West Ham player. Probably he’d have done better without missing half the club’s games through injury. As Bild.de suggest, Fullkrug has found his happy place and may never leave:

“It is possible!:Will Füllkrug stay with Werder forever?”

Runs their headline, confirming that they want to keep ‘Fulle’ even once his playing days are over:

“There have already been talks about a follow-up contract with us and the idea is still concrete. It is quite conceivable that we will tie him to the club after his career. I really appreciate his football expertise.”

Which suggests that Fullkrug has already done enough to earn another deal at Werder Bremen, meaning he’s likely never, ever coming back. Sadly, neither is the £27 million, or the nine million salary which he’s had as a West Ham player – thats three million per goal. Nice work if you can get it.

From the old Bobby Moore Upper to the Billy Bonds' stand these days - I've been watching since '03 and a supporter since about 1970.. Favourite player - Dean Ashton: Curse you Shaun Wright-Phillips. I still watch YouTube repeats of the Cup Final of 2006 hoping in vain that Shaka Hislop grows another six inches and stops Steven Gerrard's injury time equaliser. Can tell I'm getting old knowing I watched both Mark Noble's debut and his last game at West Ham. Pulling on a Claret and Blue replica shirt still makes me feel the same butterflies as when I was seven years old. Magic.

We have to demand a fee for him at the end of his fantastic goal scoring year (more goals than Kane currently) or tell him to come back. Someone will cough up say 3m for him.

Apple's new iPad mini is perfect in every way (except one)

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

For fans of the tiny tablet, this is a worthy upgrade.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

For what feels like the first time in years, Apple is in experimenting mode. From folding iPhones to touchscreen MacBooks, the company is suddenly dropping innovative products rather than incremental upgrades. But while the new iPad mini might not be the most exciting new release of 2026, for a select group of users, it might just be the best iPad yet.

The 8th generation iPad mini upgrades almost everything about the tiny tablet. The headline feature has to be the OLED display, which is brighter, crisper and more vibrant than any iPad mini display we've seen. Meanwhile, minor design changes have resulted in a thinner and lighter body – which is a much more meaningful change than it might sound.

I've always been a fan of the iPad mini. Sure, larger models like the Pro feature M-series chips and ProMotion displays, but sometimes there's no replacement for portability. As the famous saying goes, 'your best iPad is the one you have with you' – and being able to sling the iPad mini into a tote bag is invaluable. And the first thing I noticed when picking it up was the reduction in size. At 5.4mm thick and 255 grams, it's just ridiculously thin and light.

And the second thing I noticed was, yes, that OLED display. While it isn't quite as stunning as the new MacBook Pro's PROLED XDR display, those bright colours and inky blacks look stunning. For creatives, a bright, colour-accurate screen is a must, and now the smallest iPad delivers one in spades.

But there is a glaring omission. In this day and age, a 60hz refresh rate feels slow and stuttering on any device, let alone one that starts at £699. Apple's 120hz ProMotion display has made its way to most of its products by now, but it's surprising, given the scale of the rest of the updates (and the price increase) that the iPad mini hasn't been blessed with one. Reddit, as you might imagine, has not been kind.

But if you can put aside the lack of ProMotion, there's so much to love here. Performance-wise, that A20 Pro chip (which also resides in the iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone Duo) is incredibly speedy and efficient – I'm managing to eke out around ten hours on a single charge.

Another improvement is the sound quality. Apple has somehow managed to place the speakers underneath the display (or as Apple calls it, the 'acoustic display'), and the result is powerfully punchy audio for a tablet so small.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Overall, if you're somebody who already loves the iPad mini and has the cash to burn for an upgrade, the 8th generation model is a winner. Every improvement adds up to a meaningfully enhanced experience, particularly the OLED screen and the weight loss. Now, if Apple could just bring itself to give us a 120hz display, the 9th generation iPad mini might just be the perfect tiny tablet.

Daniel John is Design Editor at Creative Bloq. He has interviewed leaders and designers at brands including Apple, Microsoft and Adobe. Daniel's debut book of short stories and poems was published in 2018, and his comedy newsletter is a Substack Bestseller.

Iran War Nears End as Waller Floats 50-Bps Hike and Bitcoin Treasury Odds Jump

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

President Donald Trump says the Iran War is effectively over, but Bitcoin and financial markets face another potentially turbulent week. Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller has warned that stubborn inflation could justify a 50-basis-point rate hike in December. Meanwhile, Kalshi traders have raised the odds of a US Treasury Bitcoin purchase this year to 13%.

The three developments put geopolitics, monetary policy and Bitcoin adoption attentions being rekindled. Trump’s latest comments could ease concerns about prolonged military escalation, while Waller’s warning raises questions about borrowing costs. Bitcoin, meanwhile, is attracting attention from prediction markets betting on a possible government purchase.

Trump told reporters on Monday that the Iran War was “militarily effectively over,” suggesting Washington sees little immediate need for further military action. Speaking at the White House after meeting Qatar’s prime minister, Trump said negotiations were moving in a positive direction. He also suggested that an agreement could bring the conflict to an end fairly soon.

However, Washington has not completely ruled out further strikes. Trump said American forces remained ready to respond if circumstances changed, keeping some uncertainty around the negotiations.

Oil prices follow with a decline. Brent crude fell 5.85% last night before slipping another 1.7% to around $90.99 per barrel this morning. The retreat reflects easing concerns over prolonged supply disruptions in the Middle East.

How could it affect crypto? With the Iran war ending, cheaper oil could offer some relief for Bitcoin by reducing inflationary pressure and giving the Federal Reserve more room to eventually lower interest rates. Energy prices influence Bitcoin primarily through inflation expectations, monetary policy, and market liquidity.

Before Trump’s statement, the conflict had already created significant pressure across energy markets. Higher fuel prices have contributed to inflation concerns, complicating the Federal Reserve’s efforts to bring price growth back toward its 2% target.

An end to the Iran War could remove one important source of energy-market uncertainty.

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller has introduced another complication for investors. Just last night, Waller said the Fed could consider a 50-basis-point increase in December if October inflation data show persistent pressure in core prices.

He warned that delivering only a 25-basis-point increase could undermine the Fed’s credibility if core inflation remains stubbornly high. However, Waller currently leans toward a smaller increase if inflation improves.

FED’S WALLER BACKS DECEMBER RATE HIKE, SAYS OPEN TO 50 BPS MOVE

A 50-basis-point move would represent two standard quarter-point increases delivered in a single meeting. It would also show that policymakers remain prepared to prioritize inflation control over easing financial conditions.

The possibility matters for Bitcoin because higher interest rates generally make speculative investments less attractive. Investors may favor cash and yield-bearing assets when borrowing costs rise, and liquidity becomes more expensive.

The Iran War adds another layer to that calculation. Even if military tensions ease, energy prices and inflation could keep the Fed under pressure.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Despite the uncertain macroeconomic backdrop, prediction markets are pricing in a growing possibility of direct US government Bitcoin buying.

Kalshi odds for a US Treasury Bitcoin purchase this year have climbed to 13%. The market reflects speculation that Washington could move beyond its existing Bitcoin reserve framework and acquire additional BTC.

Bitcoin’s growing institutional ambitions are also creating interest in infrastructure designed to expand what BTC can actually do. Bitcoin Hyper (HYPER) is developing a Bitcoin Layer 2 powered by the Solana Virtual Machine (SVM). The network aims to bring Solana-style transaction speeds, low fees, and near-instant finality to Bitcoin while anchoring settlement to its underlying blockchain.

The project has already raised more than $33.2 million in presale funding, with HYPER currently priced at just $0.0136. Its testnet explorer is now live, giving users an early look at network activity. The ecosystem is designed to support Bitcoin transfers, decentralized finance, payments, and smart contracts, potentially unlocking new uses for BTC liquidity.

HYPER holders can also access advertised staking rewards of up to 35% APY through the presale. The token has a fixed supply of 21 billion, with allocations covering development, treasury, marketing, community rewards, and exchange listings.

Its planned November 26 presale conclusion is approaching, leaving buyers with a narrowing window before the next stage of the project.

With more than $33 million already raised, a live testnet explorer and the token launch approaching, Bitcoin Hyper is building momentum ahead of its next milestones. The combination of early-stage pricing, staking incentives, and Bitcoin-focused infrastructure gives the project several potential demand drivers. Its upcoming launch could become an important test of investor appetite for the next generation of Bitcoin Layer 2 networks.

For now, investors have three competing forces to watch: the possibility of an Iran War resolution, a more aggressive Federal Reserve, and growing speculation about government Bitcoin accumulation.

ChatGPT Ads is replacing two manual bid caps with average cost targets. Here's what changes for new campaigns, existing campaigns and API partners, and when.

The ChatGPT Ads platform is replacing two manual caps, the maximum bid (Max Bid) and maximum CPC (Max CPC), with average cost targets that advertisers can set as part of their advertising campaign strategy. This is according to an OpenAI help document on the topic of bidding and pricing that was updated on their website Thursday.

Beginning Nov. 9, advertisers creating a conversion campaign, which they are using to hit a specific cost per conversion, get Target CPA in place of Max Bid; likewise, advertisers creating a click campaign beginning Nov. 9 also have the option to use Target CPC. Max Bid and Max CPC will no longer apply to existing campaigns from Jan. 1, 2027.

From Nov. 18, a Max Bid or Max CPC value that an API partner sends through the old fields to create a campaign or change an ad group’s bid type becomes an average target, not a maximum.

OpenAI’s bidding page describes Target CPA and Target CPC as average cost goals rather than limits on each bid. Target CPA is the average of what an advertiser wants to spend on a conversion (such as a purchase or a lead) and Target CPC is the average for a click. In both cases, a single click or conversion may cost more or less than your target, and you may end up paying more or less on average.

For click campaigns, the page compares the new option with the cap it replaces:

“While Max CPC caps individual bids, Target CPC lets our system bid above or below your target in individual auctions, aiming to deliver as many clicks as possible from your budget while working toward your desired average CPC.”

The warning on this page is about a possible restriction on delivery when the target is set below recent performance. An example shows how they calculate a $40 actual CPA using $400 in ad spend and 10 attributed purchases. It points out that if a $20 Target CPA is set it does not mean that the system will be able to deliver purchases at a $20 cost. Additionally, the amount spent by a campaign may fall short of the full budget due to overly restrictive targets or poor conversion data.

This page says the bidding goal and the billing event type are two different settings and therefore, a Target CPA campaign can be optimized for conversions while being charged for clicks or impressions, which depends on how the campaign is being billed.

In August Brooke Osmundson reported that Microsoft would cease to offer Max CPC on new non-portfolio campaigns using a strategy such as Target CPA and Target ROAS beginning Oct. 1. However, existing campaigns and portfolio bid strategies may continue to use it. Microsoft’s official reasoning behind the change is that the setting can interfere with their bidding systems’ ability to perform optimizations toward performance objectives.

API partners are getting their own Target CPA and Target CPC fields, and the old Max Bid and Max CPC fields change behavior on Nov. 18. The bidding page says partners can adopt the new fields once they’re available but doesn’t give a date for them.

OpenAI suggests that the legacy fields be replaced with the new fields starting Nov. 18. Legacy fields will stay active until the end of December; however, if you utilize a field to create a campaign or convert an ad group’s bidding strategy, it switches to Target CPA or Target CPC and the value becomes counted as an average cost instead of a maximum bid.

On Jan. 1, 2027, legacy fields will be deprecated. As such, the page advises partners to have all integrations updated prior to this date.

OpenAI’s bidding page also explains how the price of a winning ad is set:

“The final winning auction bid sets the price you pay.”

That rule applies across all bidding strategies, including existing Max Bid and Max CPC campaigns, the page says.

The company’s advertiser basics page described a relevance-weighted, second-price auction when I read it on Nov. 5. However, that wording is gone after an update, and the page now uses the same pricing rule as the bidding page.

The basics page also still says advertisers set a maximum bid at the ad-group level, and it recommends that CPC campaigns start with a maximum bid of $3 to $5 per click. In April, I covered Digiday’s report that an early version of the ads manager showed CPC bids in that range to some pilot advertisers.

The Maximize results page is still telling advertisers who are required to operate within a bid limit that if they have to do so without an automated process, they can do it using manual or advanced bid controls. Its setup steps say to select Manual: Max bid to use a different strategy. Also, the bidding page gives the old Maximize results strategy a new name, Intelligent bidding.

With Max Bid and Max CPC, advertisers have the ability to limit how much they will bid in each auction. With Target CPA and Target CPC, it is the advertiser who sets both a budget and an average cost; then it is up to the automated bidding systems to set individual bids. Some individual clicks or conversions could come in at higher costs than the targets.

As OpenAI suggests, advertisers should consider setting their targets based upon recent actual CPA or CPC averages. In addition to reviewing the overall spend of the campaign as well as results (clicks, conversions), advertisers should also monitor the actual average cost per click or conversion as the campaign continues to learn. Advertisers who buy through API partners should find out which bid fields those tools use after Nov. 18, since a legacy max-bid value sent to create a campaign will count as an average target.

Target CPA and Target CPC are available in Ads Manager beginning Nov. 9, with advertisers able to move their campaigns from this date forward. The legacy API bid field changes behavior on Nov. 18 and is going away Jan. 1, 2027, which marks the end of Max Bid and Max CPC for those who have yet to switch.

There is no information on OpenAI’s bidding page as to what will be the target of a campaign that is still using Max Bid or Max CPC after the caps retire. By switching prior to retirement, the advertiser is able to set its new target at the time of the switch.

Felix Nmecha denies goal celebrations promote ‘intolerance, discrimination and hate’

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Celebrations linked to ultra-conservative Christian group

‘Our message is of love’ Nmecha says in reply to criticism

Borussia Dortmund and Germany midfielder Felix Nmecha has responded to criticism of goal celebrations linked to an evangelical network by saying they’ve been misunderstood and are not meant to promote “intolerance, discrimination, or hate in any form.”

Nmecha, a devout Christian, was criticised by the Dortmund official supporters’ group at the weekend for celebrating goals with a gesture signifying the “The King’s Return” campaign linked to Ballers in God (BIG), a network of Christian football players. The German public broadcaster ARD has linked BIG to ultra-conservative positions on issues including homosexuality and transgender identity.

“My celebration is not intended to promote myself or any organisation,” Nmecha wrote in a social media post on Tuesday. “Rather, it is about laying my ‘crown’ before the soon coming King and pointing to Jesus, my hope and strength. My message has never been about promoting intolerance, discrimination, or hate in any form.

“As Christians, our message is one of love and respect for everyone, regardless of where they come from, what they believe, or what their worldview may be.”

In a statement, Dortmund official supporters’ group said: “We oppose religious networks that, under the guise of faith, propagate a worldview in which individuals are denigrated or excluded based on their sexual orientation, gender identity, way of life, or personal choices”.

Dortmund said on Sunday that it understood the fans’ concerns and would speak to Nmecha. “For Borussia Dortmund, it is essential to take the concerns and needs of the fanbase seriously and to address them,” read a statement. “Naturally, we also take the current media coverage and the associated questions and discussions very seriously.”

News agency DPA reported that Jürgen Klopp, Germany’s head coach, has already spoken to Nmecha about the issue at the national team camp in Herzogenaurach, Bavaria, before upcoming Nations League games.

Warhammer Survivors Review: A Brutal New Bullet Heaven Game

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Warhammer Survivors is a brutal spin-off of Vampire Survivors, pitting heroes from Warhammer 40,000 and Age of Sigmar against endless hordes of enemies while maintaining the addictive gameplay loop.

Few games have a title as accurate or descriptive to its core content as Warhammer Survivors. In a nutshell, itt’s Vampire Survivors gameplay, but with Warhammer 40,000 and Age of Sigmar characters, monsters, and levels, but with the difficulty turned up to eleven.

Well, that’s a bit of an unfair introduction. If Warhammer Survivors was just a cosmetic upgrade, they would have just released it as DLC in Vampire Survivors, rather than giving it its own dedicated game. After all, Vampire Survivors has invited Castlevania to its roster and added Contra too, so surely Space Marines wouldn’t look out of place?

Thankfully, Warhammer Survivors shakes up the gameplay and adds new tweaks to the familiar formula that makes it a worthwhile purchase for people who aren’t familiar with the tabletop game. Long-time Vampire Survivors fans just need to be aware that its Warhammer equivalent is a fair bit harder than the content they’ve beaten before.

At its core, Warhammer Survivors has the same gameplay loop as Vampire Survivors and its many clones. The player picks a hero, who then has to survive against endless hordes of enemies. The main gameplay gimmick is that the player cannot control how the character shoots: just how they move across the battlefield.

Throughout each level, the player earns experience points from killing enemies, and when they level up, they are given new weapons and upgrades that make it easier to destroy more foes. As time goes on, the enemies get stronger and grow in numbers, meaning careful movement becomes even more important.

There are also bosses that appear throughout the stage, which are outlined in a neon green aura. Slaying these bosses results in crates being dropped with level upgrades. These are important, as if a weapon is sufficiently levelled, then a crate could carry its improved version, such as the Boltgun transforming into the Heavy Boltgun, which has a drastically improved fire rate.

Dying on a run isn’t the end of the world, as the player can find coins strewn throughout each stage that can be spent on static buffs and upgrades. These make future runs easier, so the player has a greater chance of surviving later stages.

Warhammer Survivors keeps this gameplay loop intact, though it splits everything across two worlds. On the main menu, the player selects the “Universe”: Warhammer 40,000 and Age of Sigmar, the latter of which is the modern replacement for Warhammer Fantasy (RIP). They have their own stages, characters, and enemies, with no direct crossovers between the two.

Technically, there is content that connects the two, as the upgrades unlocked are shared between the two, meaning working hard in one helps out the other. There are also non-franchise-specific levels that they share, like the Hobby Table, but for the most part, the Warhammer 40,000 and Age of Sigmar parts of the game are separate.

A new indie inspired by Vampire Survivors tasks players with defending their home computer from an onslaught of 2000s-era viruses.

So, if Warhammer Survivors is just Vampire Survivors with Ork Boyz, then is it worth buying? Well, there are some key differences that justify it existing as its own game, rather than DLC for Vampire Survivors.

In Vampire Survivors, all the weapons are tied to the player character. Warhammer Survivors changes this with its Follower system, as characters are now unlocked alongside their weapon when the player levels up. This means they follow the selected hero around, and their attacks will originate from them.

The Follower system might not seem like a big addition, but needing to account for the changing starting position of attacks makes the gameplay considerably more challenging. This is especially true for the Garlic-type weapon users, which have a static area-of-effect centred on their hero. In Warhammer Survivors, the party members move around after the player, meaning the player has to guide them, like a kite, into the enemy hordes.

The other big difference in Warhammer Survivors is how it handles end of stage bosses. In Vampire Survivors, if the player spends too long in a stage, they’ll eventually be attacked by the Grim Reaper and quickly killed. Warhammer Survivors replaces the manifestation of death with the Extremis Bosses: foes that appear at the twenty-five minute mark for an epic fight.

The Follower system might not seem like a big addition, but needing to account for the changing starting position of attacks makes the gameplay considerably more challenging.

The Extremis Bosses can’t be faced without preparation, however, as the player needs to unlock a specific item in each stage to be able to damage them. If the player doesn’t have this item, then the boss will be invincible, and it will automatically kill the hero in a cutscene.

The enemies the player faces on each stage can also have unique gimmicks, usually tied to their bosses. It’s possible for Tyranids to buff their weaker units, making them considerably faster, while the followers of Nurgle can summon massive poison-spewing chimneys, creating damaging areas for the player to walk through.

The Orks of Warhammer 40,000 easily have the best gimmick, as they can call a mighty “Waaagh”, bringing endless waves of units against the player until it ends. Surviving a Waaagh can be one of the trickiest parts of the Ork stage, unless the player prepares lots of AoE attacks to survive the horde.

The studio behind Vampire Crawlers has revealed how the game came about, thanks to its relationship with the creator of Vampire Survivors.

The new additions to Warhammer Survivors make the game considerably harder than the source game. And make no mistake: I adore Vampire Survivors and have purchased it on multiple platforms. I have endured countless runs with my boy Poe, facing the creatures of the night with nothing but a piece of Garlic and a dream, and came out the other side.

Warhammer Survivors ramps the difficulty up in a few ways. The Follower system means the origin point of your weapons keeps moving around, making it harder to accurately fire at enemies. This isn’t as much of a problem with targeted weapon users, like grenades, but AoE and shooter characters are harder to predict, as they keep moving while shooting.

The changes to bosses are also a point against the game, because Warhammer Survivors does an extremely poor job of conveying exactly what items are needed to be “Prepared” for facing the Extremis Bosses. The best clues the player gets is seeing what items are always available in each stage, as well as blanked out menus that fill out if they happen to use the correct upgrades.

There’s far too little information about what items are needed to face the Extremeis Bosses, especially as the blanked out menu only appears after a twenty-five minute run, which is hard to reach on a good day. Most players aren’t going to bother doing trial and error: they’ll just Google the answers as soon as the game launches, so why bother obfuscating it?

It also bears mentioning that the Age of Sigmar characters are way less useful than their 40,000 equivalents. The Warhammer 40,000 roster includes gun and grenade users, while the ranged Age of Sigmar characters tend to be much slower. Thankfully, the upgrades menu stays the same between rosters, so it’s possible to grind with the Warhammer 40,000 cast for stat upgrades, in order to help out their fantasy equivalents.

There are also times when the game will just kill the player with no recourse. The Age of SIgmar stages are particularly bad for this, with enemy cavalry units randomly appearing and rushing across the stage, pushing the player into a wall and instantly killing them in a way that’s impossible to avoid.

The sub bosses fought in each stage also have far, far too much health. I’ve sat there for what felt like an eternity with maxed out weapons, stunlocking a boss into place, while damage numbers just rack up, and they won’t die. It’d be fine if their gameplay made them challenging foes to match their mighty health bar, but many of them are just regular enemies with bigger sprites.

The changes to Warhammer Survivors do provide an extra layer of challenge for those coming from the source material, and I’d be lying if I said I didn’t enjoy the new additions and wrinkles: it just needs a few more tweaks and balance checks for the game to be as good as it can be.

This review covers Warhammer 40,000: Boltgun 2 and why players should play it.

The Warhammer franchise has a reputation for being extremely dark in tone, especially 40,000. The Imperium of Man isn’t exactly a lovely place to exist, between the constant threat of Xeno invasion, the corrupting influence of Chaos present in the souls of mankind, and the rampant corruption within society itself.

Warhammer Survivors is a rare example of Warhammer being tongue-in-cheek and referential. This can be seen through its many nods to the actual game and its history, especially the weapons, such as exploding d6s hitting the board, Nuln Oil slowing foes down, the measuring tape increasing damage AoE, and the experience point booster being called White Dwarf.

The highlight of this approach is the Hobby Table stage, where the player fights off monsters on a crafting table, the kind used by many real-life fans of the tabletop game. The developers had mentioned in the past that the Hobby Table stage is a nod to Micro Machines, and it shows, being easily the most fun level in Warhammer Survivors.

The visuals and overall aesthetic of Warhammer Survivors are close to that of Vampire Survivors, and like the Boltgun series, the approach is mired in retro goodness. Few Warhammer games have managed to cram as many foes onto the screen as this one, making Warhammer Survivors one of the few titles to ever capture the essence of the beloved artwork from the series, save shown through a 16-bit filter.

Warhammer Survivors doesn’t try to tell a story: it’s about throwing iconic heroes from the franchise against familiar foes. It’s clear the people who made the game are huge fans of the source material, and that love shines throughout the experience.

The new layer of difficulty in Warhammer Survivors can be frustrating at times, but it also feels necessary for helping distinguish it from Vampire Survivors. If the player can soldier through the hard moments, then they’ll get another massive dose of Survivors gameplay and content, which is likely a huge selling point on its own for people who are still addicted to the original.

Warahmmer Survivors justifies itself as a separate entity throughout plenty of new characters and stages, with enough gameplay gimmicks to ensure it’s not just a reskin. The difficulty can be frustrating at times, but people don’t sign up for a Survivors-like to have an easy time. Warhammer Survivors will satisfy people looking for more of the familiar but different enough to not be a rehash, while still paying tribute to the universes that won over fans worldwide.

2026's best game so far is finally coming to more platforms, letting mobile audiences become as hopelessly addicted as console and PC players.

Artist Sun Xun’s new animated film was made over a decade from thousands of artworks

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A scene from Sun Xun's Magic Atlas (2026) Courtesy the artist and π Animation Studio

A phrase cinephiles sometimes use to describe a visually beautiful movie is “every frame a painting”, but that is rarely more true than in Magic Atlas, a feature-length animation film by the Chinese painter Sun Xun, which was awarded a Special Jury Prize at the recently concluded Tokyo International Film Festival.

Sun spent ten years, on and off, working on the film, which he largely self-financed; rather than employ professional animators, he employed hundreds of art students (in one interview, he says 500 over the course of production) to produce individual frames, in a sort of supersized workshop model. Each frame has its own tactile quality, particularly in the sequences made in the unconventional animation medium of woodcut. When a shaft of line streams through a high window, piercing the blackness of an otherwise dark room, you can see the individual carving marks scored into the wood, which across several dozen frames dance and shimmer like the scales of a carp moving through a clear pond.

A scene from Sun Xun's Magic Atlas (2026) Courtesy the artist and π Animation Studio

Magic Atlas is a visual artist’s film, and as a storyteller, Sun thinks like a visual artist, proceeding by association, symbolism and dream logic. The magic atlas of the film’s title is a map to the mystic realms traversed by an explorer in a steampunk flying contraption. After the kaleidoscopic imagery of the film’s opening overture, he spends most of the film in one such world, a coal-mining kingdom called Luocha, where he is imprisoned on the eve of a magic festival. Maps are illegal in Luocha, as are memories. The authoritarian logic of the film is definitely allegorical, but the allegory is hard to pin down; the land’s emperor speaks of forced labour and industrial quotas, in perhaps a Maoist register, but the power behind the throne is an Orwellian cabal of anthropomorphised talking animals, who smoke cigars like colonial lords, and the state’s surveillance protocol is carried out by an army of mosquitos with cameras for heads—a swarm, if not a flock (or Flock).

In a manner similar to Chinese cinema’s emerging mystic-virtuoso Bi Gan, Sun is applying poetic ideas derived from Eastern spirituality about the oneness of all things, the many stories that are all one story. The film opens with a quote from Avatamsaka Sutra invoking “the past, the present, the future lying in the present”, and ends with a lengthy monologue, voiced by Sun himself, delivered by a hieroglyphic eagle. Its ideas about the transmigration of souls are mirrored in the mix of animation styles—the film spends most of its time in Luocha, which is rendered in monumental black-and-white woodcuts and illustrations reminiscent of Rockwell Kent, but also jumps at times to different styles that are each a world in and of themselves.

A scene from Sun Xun's Magic Atlas (2026) Courtesy the artist and π Animation Studio

There are homages to Salvador Dalí and Pieter Bruegel the Elder; a segment in riotous pastels with the Jazz Age voluptuousness of Reginald Marsh and a segment animated in crayon and magic marker by Sun’s Instagram followers during the Covid-19 pandemic; and segments informed by Sun’s own research into different styles of traditional Chinese art, including, he said in a post-screening interview, Dunhuang murals, Song dynasty painting, and multiple styles of calligraphy.

Exhibitions of Magic Atlas materials including woodcuts and concept art have shown at the Times Museum of Guangdong, and additional shows are planned in the United States (where Sun is represented by Sean Kelly Gallery). Sun received initial seed funding from Judith Neilson, the Australian philanthropist and founder of the Asian-focused White Rabbit Gallery, whose current show, up through November 15, includes Magic of Atlas – Luocha: An Infinite Journey, an 8ft-high, 52ft- long, 13-panel woodcut relief depicting images from the story in a scroll that can be stopped and started by the eyes of the viewer passing across the work—a static form of visual narrative that can be traced back to antiquity, long before still and moving images diverged.

Film-maker Lucio Castro’s new feature, “Drunken Noodles”, is set in part in a Manhattan gallery and just premiered in Cannes

Nyla Innuksuk’s “In the Heart of the South” grapples irreverently with the fraught ethics of an Inuk artist tapping into narratives of collective suffering for her work

"This World Is Not My Own", which recently premiered at South by Southwest, parallels Rowe’s life with that of the gallerist who championed her work

Las Vegas Inventory Hits Record High as Buyer Demand Sinks—but One Surprising Segment Defies the Slump

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Housing inventory in Las Vegas surged to an all-time high in October as high mortgage rates priced out everyday buyers, yet the luxury tier of the local market continued to outperform.

Active listings in Nevada's largest metro jumped nearly 10% year over year, reaching 11,447 units—the highest level on record for Realtor.com® data going back to July 2016, according to the October housing market report.

Tania Jhayem, a real estate agent at Keller Williams The Marketplace's luxury division in Las Vegas, says that mortgage rates exceeding 7% and broader affordability headwinds have encouraged buyers to be far more selective.

"After several years of an extremely competitive housing market, we're seeing a shift toward more balanced conditions, where buyers have additional choices and sellers are adjusting to a different pace," Jhayem tells Realtor.com.

New listings—a measure of fresh homes hitting the market—plunged 10.5% compared to last year, further underscoring a wider market cooldown.

Realtor.com senior economist Jake Krimmel notes that new listings accounted for less than a quarter of the overall inventory in October compared to 30.4% a year ago. The result is an inventory that is growing increasingly stale.

The typical for-sale home in Las Vegas lingered on the market for 59 days last month, two days longer than during the same period in 2025.

While the selection for sale may not be the freshest this fall—with half of the available homes sitting on the market for nearly two months—the buildup offers buyers a key advantage: time.

"Buyers who previously felt priced out or pressured into quick decisions now have more opportunities to explore their options," says Jhayem. "For sellers, proper pricing, presentation, and marketing have become increasingly important. Ultimately, we're seeing a market where both buyers and sellers are becoming more deliberate in their decisions."

Homes lingering on the market present well-prepared shoppers with opportunities to negotiate favorable terms, including price cuts, closing-cost assistance, repairs, or mortgage rate buydowns.

This fall, house hunters can also take their time to evaluate properties, review comparable sales, and make informed decisions without the intense pressure seen in recent years.

Yet, a prolonged listing history does not always spell a bargain: Some homes require major updates, have features that appeal to a small pool of would-be buyers, or are simply mispriced.

The record-setting inventory buildup was not an overnight development. Data analysis reveals that active listings in Las vegas have climbed steadily since January.

"That's a massive part of what's going on in markets like Vegas—not just in October but over the last several months as rates have risen and erased the financing advantage that buyers had enjoyed for much of this year compared to last," notes Krimmel.

The median asking price in Las Vegas registered at $459,900 in October, down 2.6% year over year but still nearly $45,000 higher than the national price. Meanwhile, contract signings were down by more than 25% year over year, outpacing the 17% decline recorded nationally.

Despite the slowdown, sellers are keeping their cool: Roughly 24% of all listings in Vegas saw price reductions, down 1.4 percentage points from last year. Mirroring the broader national trend, the dip suggests that sellers are in no rush to hand out concessions just to offload properties.

"Many of the homeowners choosing to sell today are doing so because of important life transitions rather than simply reacting to market conditions," says the Vegas agent. "We're seeing reasons to sell that include job relocations, growing families, retirees looking to downsize, estate settlements, and homeowners seeking properties that better fit their current lifestyles."

Jhayem points out that one of the important differences between today's market and previous downturns is that many local homeowners have built substantial equity, which provides greater flexibility and allows sellers to make more strategic decisions about when and how to sell.

"Homeowners with favorable mortgage rates and no immediate need to move may choose to stay put, while those ready for their next chapter are continuing to move forward," she adds.

While the broader Las Vegas housing market cooled, the upper tier heated up.

The entry threshold for the top 10% of the metro's housing market reached $1.2 million in October, up 2.2% from a year ago, marking the 10th-biggest price growth among the 43 largest metros, according to the latest Realtor.com luxury housing report.

According to Krimmel, this divergence stems from two market dynamics: robust demand from wealthy, well-capitalized buyers who are less sensitive to high mortgage rates, and the resilience of the luxury tier buoyed by sellers who can afford to wait for their target sale price.

"While the general market is maybe kind of softening, the ultraluxury market ... there's no breaks in store for it," Natalia Harris, global real estate adviser with Las Vegas Sotheby's International Realty, tells Realtor.com.

Harris points out that so far in 2026, there have been 23 properties in Vegas that were sold for over $10 million, with two more under contract. This is up from the 18 that were sold in that price range all of last year.

The adviser attributes this trend in part to Nevada's tax-friendly climate and pro-business policies, which continue to draw a steady stream of what she jokingly calls "refugees" from higher-taxation markets in California and Washington.

The other part of the equation, according to Harris, is the evolution of Las Vegas from a weekend gambling getaway destination into a prestigious sports and entertainment capital.

Jhayem agrees, stressing that year to year, Las Vegas continues to demonstrate its appeal as a luxury hub drawing deep-pocketed buyers relocating for lifestyle upgrades, tax considerations, and long-term investment goals.

"Our city offers an exceptional combination of luxury living, entertainment, dining, outdoor recreation, and relative value compared with many other major metropolitan areas," she says.

While rising luxury asking-price thresholds are an encouraging indication of the market's positioning, high-end properties are still taking longer to sell compared to average homes.

In October, the median days on the market for a luxury listing reached 73, a full week slower than a year ago. According to Jhayem, the longer wait time comes down to high net worth buyers being increasingly selective and placing a greater emphasis on quality, location, and value.

"Overall, I see Las Vegas transitioning toward a more balanced, opportunity-driven market," concludes the agent. "Buyers have greater choice and negotiating power, while sellers who approach the market strategically can still achieve successful outcomes. Today's market is less about urgency and more about making informed decisions."

Snejana Farberov is a reporter at Realtor.com covering the U.S. housing market and the latest domestic real estate trends. She has worked as a general assignment journalist in New York City and Long Island for 16 years, writing for New York Post, Daily Mail, and News 12. Snejana earned bachelor's degrees in journalism and Italian from St. John's University, followed by a master’s degree from Columbia University School of Journalism.

American Bitcoin Says Q3 Net Income Reached $126M as BTC Reserve Grew

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: American Bitcoin reported approximately $126 million in third-quarter net income and said its Bitcoin reserve increased through mining rather than new spot purchases during the period. The November 4 release also cites about $221 million in adjusted EBITDA and an approximately 5% increase in satoshis per share.

American Bitcoin reported approximately $126 million in third-quarter net income and said its Bitcoin reserve increased through mining rather than new spot purchases during the period. The November 4 release also cites about $221 million in adjusted EBITDA and an approximately 5% increase in satoshis per share. Those figures put the focus on an increasingly important distinction among public Bitcoin businesses: the difference between growing a treasury and growing the amount of Bitcoin attributable to each share.

The company says it did not issue shares through its at-the-market program or purchase Bitcoin during the quarter. Instead, management credits mining operations with reserve growth despite seasonal electricity cost pressure. It also highlights a roughly 5% increase in sats per share. This metric is worth checking because a rising total Bitcoin balance can be less impressive if the share count rises even faster. Yet sats per share is not an accounting profit measure or a guarantee of shareholder return. Investors also need to consider debt, other assets and liabilities, operating expenses and the market price paid for the stock.

American Bitcoin reported net income and adjusted EBITDA of different magnitudes. Adjusted EBITDA can help explain management's view of operating performance, but exclusions and accounting policies matter. In mining and treasury companies, the price of Bitcoin, the valuation of held assets, depreciation of mining equipment and financing arrangements can all materially affect results. A single strong quarter should therefore be read beside the company's complete financial statements and reconciliation of non-GAAP figures. Comparing adjusted EBITDA from two miners is difficult unless the underlying definitions are aligned.

Management describes producing Bitcoin at a structural discount to market prices. Miners can achieve attractive unit costs when power contracts, fleet efficiency and site economics are favorable, but network difficulty and Bitcoin's price constantly change the equation. Electricity costs can be seasonal; new mining capacity can increase competition; older machines can become less efficient. A production model that looks profitable today may be less attractive after a difficult market shift. It is useful to track the cost of mining one coin, but readers should ask exactly which direct and indirect costs are included.

Public shareholders own claims on a company, not specific coins in its reserve. That provides exposure to mining operations and management decisions as well as the Bitcoin held. The company can create value through efficient production and disciplined capital allocation, but it can also face stock-market volatility unrelated to the daily Bitcoin move. The most meaningful comparison with direct BTC ownership is not merely whether its reserves rise, but whether shareholders benefit after dilution, corporate costs and valuation premiums are considered.

The next quarter should be assessed using mined BTC, treasury changes, production cost, network difficulty, share count and cash liquidity. The company's earnings release is a useful snapshot, but it does not make future mining profitability predictable. In a sector where several operators are selling Bitcoin to fund AI data centers, American Bitcoin's stated emphasis on accumulating coins provides a distinct business model for investors to evaluate.

Primary source: American Bitcoin Corp third-quarter announcement. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s coverage of capital moving through regulated Bitcoin investments.

Mikel Arteta is keen to add a transition player to his front line

Arsenal are looking at Alex Baena, Endrick, Antonio Nusa and Junior Kroupi as potential winger signings, should they make a move in January.

Mikel Arteta wants to bolster his attack with pace after summer movement that left his forward line thin, but any deal is likely dependent on an outgoing.

There is an obvious contrast in midfield, where he has a range of options, which means a number of clubs - including Real Madrid, Atletico Madrid and Chelsea - feel a deal for Martin Zubimendi may be possible.
