Nick Cannon Defends Feud With Eminem Over Mariah Carey: ‘You Can’t Come at My Wife’

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Nick Cannon reflected on his longtime feud with Eminem in a new interview. The conflict between Cannon and the Slim Shady rapper originated in 2009 when the rapper dissed Cannon’s ex-wife Mariah Carey and took aim at Cannon on “Bagpipes From Baghdad.”

“He disrespected my woman, man,” Cannon recounted during an appearance on On Par With Maury Povich. “You can’t come at my wife and don’t expect to get this work. I think he and Mariah had a professional relationship, which he professed to be a personal relationship.”

Cannon continued, “So that was way before me. So then by the time we got married, he had some harsh words because I guess he was still in his feelings. I couldn’t stand for that at the time. At the dignity of my woman, I had to fight for my relationship, my love.”

Cannon and Carey were married in 2008, although the beef between Carey and Eminem kicked off in the early ’00s. Eminem claimed that he was involved in a romantic relationship with the singer for a few months, which she has denied. He subsequently referenced her on several songs, including “When the Music Stops” and “Superman.” In 2002, Carey seemingly referenced the situation on her song “Clown,” singing, “You should’ve never intimated we were lovers/ When you know very well we never even touched each other.”

In 2006, Eminem reignited the feud on “Jimmy Crack Corn,” which included the lyrics “Your mind’s on us like mine’s on Mariah/ And y’all are just like her, you’re all fucking liars.” He then called out her and Cannon on “Bagpipes From Baghdad.” At the time, Cannon replied to the song in a since-deleted blog post, writing, “I thought we got past the days where white men could spew vulgar obscenities at our beautiful queens and get away with it.”

Cannon released a series of diss tracks taking aim at Eminem in December 2019, after he and Carey had divorced. These included “The Invitation,” “Pray for Him,” and “Canceled: Invitation.” The following year, he explained his intention in an interview with Billboard. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

“I was defending myself once again,” Cannon said. “He keeps bringing it up for decades. I’m one of those cats that matches energy. I don’t do it — I overdo it. Even in high school, I was a little guy, but if you made fun of me, I was gonna make fun of you, your momma, and that’s the same energy of Wild ‘N Out. It was a month of social commentary, but if you’ve ever heard me speak on this matter, I always give that dude his props as an icon in hip-hop. I think he knows better now. If he keeps talking about me, I’m gonna keep talking back.”

Last year, Carey was asked about the feud during an appearance on Watch What Happens Live. Host Andy Cohen enlisted the pop diva for a game of “Plead The Fifth!,” where she could opt to remain silent on one of the three questions he asked her. “This summer, a music producer claimed the real reason why you and Eminem started beefing was because he approached you about playing his mom in 8 Mile,” Cohen inquired. “Is there any truth to that?” Trending Stories Watch Ariana Grande, Ben Stiller, Robert De Niro Take Over ‘The Tonight Show’ Pink Becomes First Solo Female Pop Artist to Headline the Sphere Paul McCartney and Ringo Starr to Reunite at Las Vegas Show in 2027 Trump Finally Gets Around to Claiming the Midterms Were Rigged

“From what I heard there is truth to that,” Carey replied. “But I don’t think that he actually—well, who knows who approached who.” Cohen responded, “But did that ignite the beef?”

“Um, no,” Carey replied. “I mean, maybe. It depends what he’s thinking. I really don’t care. Like, whatever he’s said, then I’m that, fine. Not really. But that’s a rap lyric.”

Big Walk Devs Issue Statement Over AI Usage

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

House House, the Australian development team behind indie hit Big Walk, has issued a statement via its FAQ page detailing a previously undisclosed use of AI "in the final stretch" of the game's development.

This comes following a "voluntary disclosure" as part of the game's submission for next year's IGF Awards. Earlier, a selection of IGF jurors issued a signed statement signalling their intention not to vote "for any game that uses generative AI in any capacity."

Specifically, House House says LLMs (Large Language Models) were used "sparingly [...] to help work through difficult technical challenges and bugs." The team estimates that "well under 0.1% of Big Walk’s code had anything to do with this usage," and stresses that "generative AI was not used in any other context when making the game."

While Steam requires a generative AI disclosure on its storefront, the platform "does not request disclosure of code or chatbot usage."

House House provides an example of an issue that caused the game to "run 50% slower for around 20 seconds at sunrise and sunset":

"We researched the problem extensively, and we looked at the URP code ourselves, but we couldn’t find a way. Then, we were able to speak directly to an engineer at Unity, who asked around and ultimately concluded that what we wanted wasn’t possible. Finally, we posed the question to an LLM chatbot. After a few dead ends, it suggested a possible fix, and while we had to modify the suggestion extensively, it worked. This fix represented about 10 lines of code inside URP."

The developer goes on to say that this usage is "not an endorsement" of AI tools and that the developers "support the work of those who are trying to limit the harm generative AI is doing to videogames and our world, and apologise that Big Walk’s LLM usage may not meet their standards."

The lack of an internal AI policy was a "failure", according to the statement, and the team intends to "have a more comprehensive policy going forwards."

Here's House House's statement on its LLM usage in full (thanks, Aftermath):

In the final stretch of Big Walk’s development, LLMs were used sparingly by House House to help work through difficult technical challenges and bugs. In some cases, snippets from LLM responses were copied as a starting point to be modified into game code—we’d estimate that well under 0.1% of Big Walk’s code had anything to do with this usage, and generative AI was not used in any other context when making the game.

The primary use case for House House’s use of LLMs was to research, via chatbots, issues we encountered with third-party code. If you’re technically-minded, and curious, here’s an example. We needed to cull shadow casters by distance from the camera, but Unity URP only allows culling shadow casters by distance in camera forwards, so shadow casters behind the player were being rendered at an infinite distance. This made our game run 50% slower for around 20 seconds at sunrise and sunset. We researched the problem extensively, and we looked at the URP code ourselves, but we couldn’t find a way. Then, we were able to speak directly to an engineer at Unity, who asked around and ultimately concluded that what we wanted wasn’t possible. Finally, we posed the question to an LLM chatbot. After a few dead ends, it suggested a possible fix, and while we had to modify the suggestion extensively, it worked. This fix represented about 10 lines of code inside URP.

We originally wrote a version of the above disclosure as it was a suggested part of our IGF submission. We have not made any disclosure on Steam as the platform does not request disclosure of code or chatbot usage. We are posting this disclosure as it has become a matter of public interest.

This is not an endorsement of, or argument for, the use of AI tools, however minimal. We have some concern that voluntary disclosures like this might contribute to the normalisation of this technology, or its treatment as “inevitable”. We support the work of those who are trying to limit the harm generative AI is doing to videogames and our world, and apologise that Big Walk’s LLM usage may not meet their standards.

Big Walk was developed without an internal AI usage policy in place, and we consider that lack of policy a failure. We will work to have a more comprehensive policy going forwards.

For further context, here's the earlier statement put out by a select group of IGF jurors, including Austin Walker and Sam Barlow:

Last month, House House published a separate statement explaining that the Palestinian-led BDS boycott against Microsoft is the reason the developer isn't publishing Big Walk on Xbox platforms.

House House was previously best known for its 2019 hit Untitled Goose Game. Big Walk launched in August 2026 to critical acclaim and reached one million sales in less than a week.

Andrade double helps FC Köln come from behind to beat RB Leipzig 3-1

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

To help deliver timely coverage of women's football, we may use AI tools to support content creation and editing. While we strive for accuracy and editorial quality, readers should independently verify important information.

FC Köln beat RB Leipzig 3-1 in the Frauen-Bundesliga on Monday 9 November, overturning a first-half deficit in Cologne. It was their third straight win over Leipzig, after a 1-0 victory in the DFB-Pokal on 31 October and a 1-0 league win in Leipzig on 21 December 2025.

Leipzig went ahead in the 20th minute without needing a finish of their own. Schmitz turned the ball into her own net to give the visitors a 1-0 lead.

Köln responded just before the break. In the 38th minute Andrade levelled from a Bremer assist. Two minutes later Leimenstoll scored the goal that proved to be the winner, set up by Imping.

Leipzig’s Schimmer was booked for dissent in the 41st minute. The home side went in 2-1 up at half-time.

The third goal arrived in the 57th minute. Degen provided the assist and Andrade added her second of the night, finishing with a rating of 8.92, the highest on the pitch, from 65 minutes of football. Degen was later shown a yellow card for a foul in the 63rd minute.

The numbers matched the scoreline. Köln had 22 shots to Leipzig’s 8, with 15 of theirs from inside the box, and built 2.29 expected goals against 0.52.

Finishing could have been sharper still. Fourteen of Köln’s attempts went wide, and they were caught offside 6 times. They also won 10 corners to Leipzig’s 6 and had 53% of the ball.

Leipzig did test the home goalkeeper. They put 5 shots on target and all 5 were saved, so their only goal came from Schmitz’s own goal.

Leimenstoll added 4 key passes to her goal and was rated 7.32. Bremer’s assist came from a player who already has 4 league goals this season, joint fifth in the Frauen-Bundesliga scoring chart.

Die Frauen des 1. FC Köln gewinnen ihr Heimspiel gegen RB Leipzig souverän mit 3:1. Das Ergebnis hätte durchaus höher ausfallen können, aber stand leider 2x das Aluminium im Weg.#effzeh #effzehFrauen pic.twitter.com/Atwokv4z6Z

— Büdchen_Micha (@schoti75) November 9, 2026

Köln are sixth with 15 points from 9 games, a record of 5 wins and 4 defeats with a goal difference of 3. Werder Bremen and Bayer Leverkusen sit just above them on 16 points.

Leipzig are ninth on 10 points. This was their third defeat in a row in all competitions, following the cup exit in Cologne and a 1-5 loss to Bayern Munich. Before that run they had won three straight, against SC Freiburg, Mainz 05 and Blau-Gelb Marburg.

The turnaround in this fixture is marked. Leipzig won the meetings on 1 September 2024, 16 December 2024 and 6 September 2025, and have now lost the last three.

Both sides play again on Sunday 15 November. Köln travel to Nürnberg, who are 12th, while Leipzig host third-placed Eintracht Frankfurt.

Robert De Niro's Ex Toukie Smith Dead at 74 After Battling Multiple Sclerosis

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Model and actress Toukie Smith, the ex-girlfriend of Robert De Niro and the mother of his twins, has died at age 74 after battling multiple sclerosis.

Smith’s two children with De Niro, Airyn and Julian, confirmed their mother’s death to The Hollywood Reporter on Monday, November 9.

Smith died Wednesday, November 4, in New York after a “long, courageous battle” with MS, Airyn and Julian, both 31, said.

The twins said that their mom “shared in a remarkable family legacy that helped shape American fashion and culture,” adding that “her beauty, grace and unmistakable presence helped pave the way for generations of Black models and designers in fashion.”

“Toukie was a force of nature,” Airyn and Julian said. “She left an indelible mark on our hearts and on everyone lucky enough to know her.”

Smith was a highly recognized model and also appeared in the NBC sitcom 227.

De Niro, 83, and Smith were live-in partners from 1988 to 1996. They welcomed Airyn and Julian in 1995 via a surrogate.

De Niro also shares adopted daughter Drena, 59, and son Raphael, 50, with his first wife, Diahnne Abbott, son Elliott, 28, and daughter Helen, 14, with ex-wife Grace Hightower, and daughter Gia, 3, with current girlfriend Tiffany Chen.

In April 2025, Smith and De Niro’s daughter, Airyn, came out as a transgender woman.

“Obviously no parent is perfect, but I am grateful that both my parents agreed to keep me out of the limelight. They wanted it very private,” Airyn told Them in a profile published at the time. “They have told me they wanted me to have as much of a normal childhood as possible.”

De Niro publicly expressed his support for his daughter, telling Variety in a statement, “I loved and supported Aaron as my son, and now I love and support Airyn as my daughter. I don’t know what the big deal is. I love all my children.”

In an October 2025 interview with Them, Airyn shared how her Oscar-winning father had supported her through her transition.

“The support has still been nonstop from him, and I’m so grateful,” she said. “He was talking about how happy he was for me because I have an amazing boyfriend who he’s met, and [he said], ‘You have somebody who loves you for you, and you don’t need to change yourself for anybody.’”

Airyn also opened up about discussing her transition during a therapy session with her father.

“He said, ‘There’s no other thing to do than accept your kid for who they are and where they decide to go in life,’” she recalled. “He’s very good at being a dad and, not just that, [but also] accepting people from all walks of life.”

Opinion: Liverpool have found their next Mo Salah, he's going to be incredible

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have found their next Mo Salah, and he's going to be incredible.

Nobody wanted things to come to an end this summer. Things had gone badly for Salah in his final season at Anfield.

The conflict between him and Arne Slot ultimately forced him to make a decision he might no have made otherwise.

With the benefit of hindsight. Knowing both Slot and Richard Hughes as well as Michael Edwards left the club, Salah might have opted to stay put.

However, he made the decision to leave and agreed with Liverpool to rip up the final year on his contract in return for a new adventure.

The Egyptian opted to find that adventure in Turkey, where he was welcomed like a hero in Trabzonspor and the rest is history.

Liverpool are missing Mo SalahUltimately, the Reds then spent the summer trying to replace Salah but to no avail. The club's main target, Yan Diomande, opted to favour Paris Saint Germain's advances over the Reds and then he moved to Real Madrid. Yankuba Minteh and Ismaila Sarr were targeted as right-sided wingers but Liverpool never pulled the trigger on those moves deeming them too expensive. It means the Reds now have four natural left-wingers and no recognised right-winger in the squad - except for 16-year-old Joshua Abe. At the same time, Salah is flourishing in Trabzonspor. The 34-year-old looks quick and he's beating defenders for fun. On top of that, he also has 16 goal contributions in 16 games for the club. Liverpool could do with someone of his quality right now.

Ring’s new drone patrols your home to keep an eye on things – even upstairs

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Home & Office > Smart Home > Home Security

Alongside the Ring Drone Cam, Ring introduced the Rover Cam for businesses. It investigates disturbances and streams 4K footage.

Today, Ring announced the launch of a new subbrand, Ring Robotics, with two new products: the $249 Ring Drone Cam, an indoor “mobile security camera” drone that shoots 2K video, and the $999 Ring Rover Cam, a security robot for businesses that patrols autonomously and investigates disturbances.

Also: Amazon’s new Echo Studio gives Alexa+ an AI camera to see inside your home

Both devices use Ring’s custom navigation software, “AI Pilot,” to autonomously navigate interior spaces and move between set points along a patrol, rather than being controlled directly by the user. AI Pilot leverages the devices’ cameras, sensors, and vision models, along with cloud processing, to create interior maps, then move the devices between predesignated patrol points such as a side door or a window.

I recently saw both new robots in person at a showcase in New York hosted by Ring’s founder and chief inventor, Jamie Siminoff. They certainly do what they advertise but come with some significant limitations. The drone, for example, can only stay in the air for about 8 minutes before it needs to dock and recharge, while the Ring Rover Cam is slow and deliberate, seemingly ideal for routine surveillance rather than rapid response to an event.

The Ring Drone Cam moves around predetermined patrol points in your home, sending you a live 2K video feed whether you’re just in another room or out of town. The user doesn’t actually fly the drone; instead, it moves around autonomously using Ring’s custom “AI Pilot” navigation technology, leveraging its cameras, sensors, and cloud processing to create a map of its surroundings. This allows it to fly through the air at an appropriate height, going from room to room, even up stairs.

In the Ring app, the user creates patrol points they want the drone to move between — likely a window, doorway, or high-traffic room — creating a set path that is both predictable (so you always know where the drone will go) and relevant, so it’s only monitoring the areas you want it to. But here is where its limitations come into play: It needs a finite path to navigate because its flight time is limited to just five to eight minutes in the air before it has to return to its charging dock.

That makes the dock both the start and end point for the drone’s routine trips, and it’s designed with this in mind. When it goes to land, the drone hovers over the dock and lowers itself down, dropping into place so it can start charging automatically. I asked about its potential to land elsewhere, and Ring told me it would only make an emergency landing outside of its dock in case it didn’t have enough power to get home.

Also: Amazon’s new Echo Dot can turn into a Wi-Fi extender – how it works

There’s one other very important caveat: This thing is loud. There’s no sneaking up on anyone with the Ring Drone Cam, as the entire household is likely to hear it the second it undocks. The four propellers emit a constant, high-pitched whirring while in the air that’s loud enough to announce its presence to anyone in the vicinity: intruder or resident.

During the product briefing, Siminoff insinuated that this was part of its design to dissuade home intruders snooping around your house at night (especially when paired with the drone’s front-facing spotlight), but it’s likely just the result of its engineering, especially considering this is a first-gen product. It’s so loud that it makes me think it’s better suited to navigating the home when no one’s home (although whether or not your pets go bonkers trying to catch it is a whole other issue).

The Ring Drone is designed to work out of the box with the existing Ring ecosystem, including the 2K Video Doorbell and Indoor Cam, and is also compatible with Amazon’s Map View.

The Ring Drone Cam will start shipping on Jan. 27, 2027, and will cost $249.

The other product Ring announced is the Rover Cam, a mobile robot with a 4K camera designed to monitor large spaces like warehouses, offices, or businesses. Ring calls it a “security guard that never clocks out,” but it’s more like a slow-moving droid that goes on set patrol paths. It leverages the same AI Pilot technology as the Ring Drone to map its surroundings and navigate around shelving, furniture, and obstacles in real time.

It’s much quieter than its airborne sibling, traveling at low speeds and offering mobile surveillance instead of wiring every square inch of a large space. If the technology here looks familiar, it’s because it resembles the Amazon Astra, a smaller robot designed for home monitoring but with a very similar build.

The idea here is to deploy the robot to certain areas of a workplace via the app, then get 360-degree views once it’s there. Like the drone, it can also set out on automated routes at select timing intervals — like right after a business closes or before it opens. During working hours, it chugs along with its camera, capturing everything in its view.

Also: The 4 biggest Amazon smart home announcements from today

If another Ring device is triggered, the Rover Cam autonomously goes to investigate the disturbance, providing 4K footage in real time via the app. It also features a periscope camera that can extend to see over obstacles like shelving, counters, or furniture, providing a high-level view if needed. In case you lose it in a large warehouse or office, it has a “light tower” that projects light upward onto the ceiling, notifying users of its location.

Even when docked, it uses motion-sensing technology to start recording the second it senses any kind of movement, just like Ring doorbell cameras. In case of an emergency, the Rover features a siren and red flashing lights to dissuade intruders.

The Ring Rover Cam is available for preorder now and will start shipping on Dec. 16, 2026. It will cost $999.

Leopards, it is said, do not change their spots. Neither, it would seem, do out-of-favour ‘wantaways’ who refuse to play for one manager having fallen out with his predecessors, despite being made a millionaire by the club that employs him, change their mindsets.

Such would seem to be the case with the talented yet angst-ridden package that is Jean Clair Todibo, once of Barcelona, now of West Ham yet loaned out through sheer frustration to Lens back in his native France.

Yet Todibo hasn’t managed to make the mindset leap towards peace, contentment, and a regular starting slot.

It’s all relayed by insidefutbol.com in their article

…Lens boss Yannick Cahuzac has played down West Ham United loanee Jean-Clair Todibo being angry about being substituted off against Lille and insists the defender’s fitness is being worked on.

On Friday, against Lille, the West Ham centre-back started the game and he was substituted in the 62nd minute mark, a decision which left the player visibly frustrated and angry. “

Todibo now on loan at French side Lens but still not pulling up any trees: Is anyone surprised?

Revealing that even now, two and a half years since Todibo’s arrival at West Ham, the player is still not fit for a full game. Surprise, surprise.

Whether he will ever fully solve his ‘fitness issues’ after his dreadful childhood accident, who knows. His very existence near the top of the football pyramid is testament to his enormous mental strength. Ring’s Flying Home Security Drone Is Finally Here, and It’s Surprisingly Affordable

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Ring's $250 flying indoor security camera is set to arrive in January. We got a first look at Ring Drone in action.

Ring Home Security has taken flight, and the brand’s tiny home security drone is surprisingly affordable. The $250 mini indoor drone camera was announced on Tuesday as part of the brand’s new arm, Ring Robotics. Ring Drone uses a learned map of your home to zoom around autonomously for up to 8 minutes at a time with custom routes to record places a couple of fixed cameras can’t.

The small drone weighs just 5.8 ounces and houses a pair of 2K cameras mounted on the front and bottom, respectively. The cameras use bright LED lights to enable day or night monitoring, sending a live feed or recorded video to your device. The LEDs are also meant to ward off potential intruders peering in from the outside.

You can program the flying cam to make regular flights, sending it on target missions to check on the dog or see if the stove was left on. The Ring Drone also syncs with other Ring cameras and sensors to automatically respond when windows and doors are opened at certain hours.

If you’re thinking, “wait, didn’t Ring already launch a drone camera?” you’d be right — sort of. The brand “launched” the Ring Always Home Cam in 2020. The $250 indoor drone saw a limited invitation-only program but made it to public release. We’ve been assured this latest iteration is ready for primetime, though it’s still not available for preorder on Amazon.

Ring founder and CEO Jamie Siminoff demoed the drone, which has been in development for more than five years, in an event space set up to replicate a home. During the demo, the small drone lifted gently from the dock and rose to about eye level where it hovered as if to say “hello” to the group of journalists gathered around.

Once airborne, the drone turned toward the adjacent room and whirred off to investigate. After a few seconds of scanning, the drone returned to the first room, docked and resumed charging.

While in flight, the Ring Drone made a notably loud buzz reminiscent of a golf cart in reverse. Siminoff quickly noted that the sound was an intentional choice meant to deter potential intruders and alert home dwellers to the drone’s presence.

The 1-minute flight was enough to cover two medium-sized rooms, though the drone can fly up to eight minutes on a full charge before needing about an hour to recharge. Ring reps told me it can monitor a home up to 3,500 square feet – more than enough for most – but Siminoff says they plan to improve the range over time.

If you’re worried about the drone knocking over vases and flying into ceiling fans like I was, Siminoff assured us the preprogrammed coverage zone is tight.

The drone is powered by AI Pilot, “a custom-built system that uses the device’s cameras, specialized vision models, sensors and cloud processing to see, think, and navigate autonomously.” (We’ll see for ourselves once we get a unit in for testing.)

To create your drone’s fly zone, you carry it through the space where it intelligently identifies rooms and suggests patrol points like doors, windows or a nursery. You can add rooms and areas based on your home’s layout and security preferences.

The $250 Ring Drone Camera isn’t yet available for preorder, but Ring representatives at the launch event assured us it will be soon, and units are set to begin shipping in late January 2027. We’ll update this story when it becomes available for preorder or purchase.

Cubarsí wins 2026 Golden Boy with 475 of 500 points as Barça reach five winners

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Barcelona centre-back Pau Cubarsí, 19, has won Tuttosport's 2026 Golden Boy award for Europe's best under-21 player by a wide margin. The landslide puts a defender on top of a prize rarely won by one, and pushes Barcelona further clear as the club with the most winners.

This year’s vote was one-sided. No other candidate came close to the Barcelona defender.

The margin shows how settled the vote was. It also marks a rare win for a player in his position. Corriere dello Sport described him as only the second defender ever to win it. He succeeds PSG’s Désiré Doué, the 2025 winner.

Cubarsí now joins a club list that already featured Messi, Pedri, Gavi and Lamine Yamal. Four of the five have come in the last six years. Pedri won in 2021, Gavi in 2022 and Lamine Yamal in 2024.

No player can win the award twice. Each Barcelona success therefore means a different young player reaching the top of the vote. The recent run points to a steady flow of talent into the first team.

The award caps a strong year for the defender. He was a big part of Barcelona’s LaLiga and Super Cup wins. He was also named young player of the tournament as Spain won the World Cup. He started all eight of Spain’s games there. FIFA’s Young Player Award made him the first defender to receive it. Spain beat Argentina 1-0 after extra time in the final. They kept seven clean sheets and conceded only once, in a 2-1 quarter-final win over Belgium. The Golden Boy arrives just weeks after he narrowly missed out on the Kopa Trophy.

Cubarsí was not the only Barcelona name on the list. Three of Monday’s winners came from the club. Joan Laporta took the Best President award. Barça Femení B’s Giulia Galli won the Best Italian Golden Girl prize. Outside the club, the Best Agent prize went to Jorge Mendes.

Laporta’s prize puts the club’s president alongside its young defender in the same announcement. Galli’s award extends the club’s haul to its women’s set-up. Together, the three prizes give Barcelona recognition across the boardroom, the men’s first team and the women’s game.

— FC Barcelona (@FCBarcelona) November 9, 2026

The timing suits both club and country. Barcelona sit top of LaLiga. Spain have a decider against England coming up. For Cubarsí, the award adds individual recognition to a season already built on trophies.

The prizes will be handed out at a ceremony in Turin on 14 December. All three of Barcelona’s awards become official at that ceremony.

Coachella 2027 Lineup Leaks: Polymarket Odds Reveal the Top 3 Favorites

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Home » Crypto Market News » Coachella 2027 Lineup Leaks: Polymarket Odds Reveal the Top 3 Favorites

Industry leaks point to major pop and rock icons—here is who prediction markets expect to officially take the stage.

Rumors about the Coachella 2027 lineup picked up steam after reports and social media posts pointed to SZA, Dua Lipa, and Tame Impala as the festival’s possible headliners.

While the music festival’s promoter Goldenvoice has yet to confirm any performers, crypto-based prediction markets reacted instantly to the industry buzz.

On Polymarket, the leading Web3 prediction platform, capital flowed rapidly into event contracts as crypto traders rushed to price in the latest reporting.

Industry buzz began picking up momentum after reports from media outlets, including TMZ, indicated that talks were underway with Dua Lipa and Tame Impala.

The speculation spiked further when R&B star SZA seemingly corroborated the rumors online, pointing toward a headlining trio representing three distinct corners of modern pop, R&B, and psychedelic rock.

Reports also suggest that festival organizers had approached other massive acts—including BTS, Oasis, Radiohead, and Rage Against the Machine—before cementing focus on the current trio.

Crypto-based prediction markets have become a real-time barometer of public sentiment when entertainment leaks surface. The rumors have also played out on Polymarket, where odds shift instantly as new information emerges.

Polymarket’s latest odds show strong confidence in the leaked trio.

As of November 10, SZA has a 74% implied probability of appearing on the lineup. Dua Lipa follows at 65%, with $9,917 in trading volume—the highest individual total among all listed artists—marking a potential milestone for her first solo Coachella headlining performance. Tame Impala sits close behind at 61%.

Beyond the top three, other artists are also drawing bets. BLACKPINK has a 45% implied probability, followed by Drake at 28%.

Other names further down the list include Travis Scott at 16%, Harry Styles at 15%, Daft Punk at 13%, BTS at 7%, and Justin Bieber at 5%.

Trading volume across Coachella-related markets climbed within hours of the rumors spreading on social media. The market is scheduled to remain open until January 31, 2027.

The reaction to the Coachella 2027 lineup is a prime example of how prediction markets react to mainstream entertainment news long before official announcements drop. Traders aggregate information from media reports, social media leaks, and industry chatter to price in probabilities.

While these odds do not represent guaranteed confirmation from the promoter Goldenvoice, they offer a direct view into market consensus—one that shifts dynamically as fresh details emerge.

One of the world’s biggest music festivals is no stranger to blockchain. In 2022, Coachella auctioned ten lifetime-pass NFTs. The collection raised roughly $1.4 million. After FTX’s collapse that November, many holders lost access to their keys, as assets kept on the exchange became stuck.

In 2024, the festival tried again with OpenSea on Avalanche, reissuing NFTs to affected buyers, though demand for the first new collection was initially weak.

Coinbase Opens Tokenized Gold-Powered Loans

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Home » Blitz news » Coinbase Opens Tokenized Gold-Powered Loans

The gold-backed loans run on a new USDC/PAXG market on Morpho Blue, deployed on Base.

Eligible Coinbase users in the United States can now borrow USDC using Paxos’s PAX Gold as collateral. The feature, which went live in early November, marks the first time Coinbase Borrow has accepted a non-crypto asset.

Until now, Coinbase Borrow had only accepted crypto-native collateral such as Bitcoin, Ether, XRP, and Solana. PAXG, each token representing one fine troy ounce of physical gold held in LBMA-accredited vaults, becomes the first real-world asset on the platform.

Millions of Coinbase users can now borrow USDC against Paxos's tokenized gold, powered by Morpho.Gold is the first non-crypto collateral in Coinbase Borrow, which has ~$3.4B of collateral and $1.7B+ in loans on Morpho.@Morpho pic.twitter.com/CpwST0wJnJ

Users can pledge the token inside the Coinbase app and receive USDC without selling their gold position.

Coinbase supplies the user interface and customer base; Morpho supplies the on-chain lending infrastructure and liquidity.

The same integration already supports roughly $3.4 billion in collateral and more than $1.7 billion in active loans across crypto assets. Adding gold required no new lending stack—only a new market on the existing system.

Borrowers face variable interest rates set by the Morpho market rather than by Coinbase. There is no fixed repayment schedule or minimum payment.

*COINBASE & PNC EXPAND STABLECOIN PAYMENT PARTNERSHIP*COINBASE: ELIGIBLE PNC CLIENTS CAN RECEIVE PAYMENTS IN USDC

Positions are governed by loan-to-value thresholds: if the value of the pledged PAXG falls too far relative to the outstanding USDC, the collateral can be liquidated.

Access is limited to eligible U.S. customers outside New York, with asset-specific borrowing limits and terms displayed in the app.

Only a few billion dollars of the roughly $30 trillion gold market has been tokenized so far. Coinbase’s move gives holders of one of the larger regulated gold tokens a way to raise dollar liquidity against it without leaving the exchange.

AVAX NYSE Deal Sparks 10% Rally: Avalanche Wins Tokenized Stock Role

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Avalanche (AVAX) climbed on Tuesday after the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) selected its blockchain technology to power settlement for a new tokenized securities platform. The move gives Avalanche a significant role in Wall Street’s push toward blockchain-based financial infrastructure.

AVAX price reached $8.60, gaining 5.2% over 24 hours. The token had climbed as high as $8.99, putting the psychological $9 resistance level back within reach.

Trading activity also picked up sharply. Avalanche recorded $537 million in 24-hour trading volume, an increase of nearly 448% from the previous day. The announcement followed a rally of as much as 10% on Monday, when AVAX outperformed most major coins.

Avalanche would provide the core ledger technology for the clearing firm supporting NYSE’s upcoming digital alternative trading system (ATS). The platform aims to support 24-hour trading, clearing, and settlement of tokenized securities. Investors could eventually trade tokenized US stocks and exchange-traded funds outside traditional market hours.

Avalanche will provide the underlying blockchain infrastructure through a dedicated network built around institutional requirements. Meanwhile, NYSE will retain its existing Pillar matching engine to process buy and sell orders.

The announcement shows Avalanche’s growing appeal among traditional financial institutions seeking faster and more flexible settlement infrastructure.

The timing is particularly interesting as Wall Street increasingly explores tokenization. Nasdaq is developing its own tokenized securities infrastructure, while the Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation (DTCC) is advancing blockchain-based settlement initiatives.

NYSE’s decision gives Avalanche a prominent position in that competition. The exchange operates one of the world’s largest stock markets, making the partnership a significant endorsement of Avalanche’s technology.

AVAX price is approaching a critical technical level after yesterday’s explosive rally. The token climbed from an intraday low near $7.82 toward $8.99, putting $9 within striking distance. A decisive breakout could open the door toward $9.10, the next resistance area.

Trading volume provides another encouraging signal. The sharp increase suggests renewed interest in AVAX as investors react to the NYSE announcement. If buyers maintain momentum above $9, Avalanche could extend its recovery toward higher resistance levels. Conversely, $8 remains the first important psychological support if traders take profits.

The rally also arrives as Avalanche works to strengthen its position in institutional finance. Its blockchain infrastructure is increasingly competing for attention alongside established networks seeking to capture the growing tokenized asset market.

The NYSE announcement gives that narrative considerable weight. Rather than simply attracting another crypto partnership, Avalanche has secured a role in the infrastructure plans of a major traditional stock exchange.

How AI security failures are endangering your supply chain – and what you can do

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

New research provides a stark look at the AI supply chain and the risks it brings to businesses.

Artificial intelligence companies worldwide are bringing a new wave of vulnerabilities and security issues to the digital supply chain, researchers warn.

According to a new report published by Black Kite on Tuesday, there now exists a “widespread cybersecurity weakness” across the “rapidly expanding ecosystem of companies developing, deploying, and supporting artificial intelligence.”

AI and smart solutions don’t automatically equate with safe solutions. In a study titled “2026 AI Supply Chain Report: The Risks Behind Your AI Relationships,” Black Kite says that many AI suppliers — the companies that develop and deploy AI services to today’s businesses — are struggling with “basic security practices.”

Also: Rogue AI incidents hit ‘tens of thousands’: Can businesses trust these tools?

According to the research, after examining the companies themselves, along with their software, integrations, and components, researchers found failures to patch critical vulnerabilities, protect credentials, authenticate users and email, and address network security issues.

The study analyzes 18,160 AI companies worldwide, ranging from 0-10 employees to more than 1,000 employees. A company’s AI involvement was determined through reviews of company products and services and primary business activities, covering six broad groups: everyday application development; AI development and implementation; AI applications in specialized sectors such as healthcare, IT, and finance; industrial organizations; and AI foundations working on data, compute, and modeling.

A small percentage of assessed companies are considered “unclassified,” and the study excluded businesses that use AI only incidentally.

In addition, unusual network activity was detected at 44.3% of organizations, and botnet-related activity was recorded at 41.8% of AI companies.

AI’s main business appeal is its ability to integrate with existing software and platforms to handle manual tasks, heavy lifting, and tedious, time-consuming work, freeing employees to focus on work that requires human oversight.

Enterprises are investing billions of dollars in AI. Menlo’s 2026 research estimates that enterprise AI spend reached $37 billion in 2025, with half of overall spend going to AI apps. In total, 76% of enterprise AI solutions are purchased rather than built internally.

However, the moment an AI or automated component is introduced into a corporate ecosystem or operational platform, any security problems it has impact the user — and each additional integration, such as software components or open-source tooling, may also increase the attack surface.

In an accompanying analysis of AI firms’ use of Model Context Protocol (MCP) components, which can link AI apps to business systems, 48.8% of 1,732 logged security findings were rated High or Critical, including code and command injection vulnerabilities.

Also: The new enterprise AI expert every company needs

It’s not just the AI application itself. As noted by Black Kite, AI suppliers often have access to customer data, developer environments, business infrastructure, and enterprise platforms. If the supplier’s credentials are stolen, this could expose its customers to a range of cyberattacks.

The researchers found, for example, that 19.9% of AI companies offering solutions for enterprise AI deployment had corporate login information in infostealer records, and that stolen corporate logins, overall, were linked to 6.8% of AI suppliers in the study.

Visibility matters, too, for businesses that should be monitoring their digital supply chains. However, 33.2% of AI companies assessed have a “limited” digital footprint, including their domains, subdomains, and IP addresses.

The same principles for protecting the digital supply chain apply to AI suppliers, where continual monitoring and assessment are among the best protections businesses have.

Staying informed and aware of the risks to your own business makes all the difference. As recommended by Black Kite, it takes more than a single point-in-time security assessment — frequent monitoring and engaging with suppliers to understand their technologies and dependencies can reveal potential security issues.

Also: Do you need AI agent liability insurance?

If you find weaknesses, manage them by priority, handling ransomware-related exposures and vulnerabilities actively exploited in the wild first.

Also audit an AI tool’s integrations and components, access, what data it can reach, and the potential impact of exploitation. Once you’re armed with this information, it’s possible to weigh risk and reward — and whether the AI tool is worth the potential attack exposure it brings. If you decide to keep it, limit its permissions and access to what it requires.

Rob Reiner's Children Make Rare Public Appearance to Honor Parents

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Rob Reiner and Michele Singer Reiner’s children, Jake and Romy Reiner, made a rare public appearance to honor their parents nearly one year after the filmmaker and his wife were killed.

On Sunday, November 8, Jake, 35, and Romy, 28, presented family friend Jamie Lee Curtis the inaugural Michele and Rob Reiner Champion for Change Award at the Children Institute’s eighth annual Cape & Gown Gala in Beverly Hills, California. The ceremony celebrates “individuals whose humanitarianism has helped put opportunity within reach for thousands of children and families,” according to the awards’ website.

TMZ reported that Jake told the crowd that his parents were always looking to make the world a better place, describing the loving environment they created for their own children at home.

The event raised $4 million for the Children’s Institute, per TMZ.

Rob and Michele were found dead in their Brentwood, California, home in December 2025. They were 78 and 70, respectively.

The couple’s son, Nick Reiner, was arrested in connection with their murders later in December. He was indicted on two counts of first-degree murder in August. He has pleaded not guilty and is awaiting trial.

In September, Los Angeles District Attorney Nathan Hochman confirmed that prosecutors will not seek the death penalty against Nick, 33, who has struggled with addiction and mental health issues over the years.

“After a careful and thorough review of all these factors, the district attorney’s office will not be seeking the death penalty in this case,” Hochman told NBC News at the time. “That means that the maximum sentence that Mr. Reiner will be looking at is life without the possibility of parole.”

“We did speak with Nick Reiner’s siblings, and they made their views very clear,” he added. “We took their views into consideration in ultimately deciding not to seek the death penalty.”

Last week, Curtis, 67, shared how she and husband Christopher Guest are supporting Jake and Romy following their parents’ deaths.

“Of course, I mean, we are family,” Curtis told Extra. “Something like this brings people so much closer. Like Jake and I were sitting shoulder-to-shoulder doing an interview…. and just feeling him there is emotional, and then of course him talking about his parents and their fantastic relationship.”

In August, Jake opened up about processing the deaths of his parents, as well as his brother’s arrest.

“It’s been eight months. It feels like eight years at the same time, and it also feels like eight minutes,” Jake told KABC-TV’s Mark Cota-Robles in an interview that aired on Good Morning America. “Every moment that I wake up, I’m reminded of my reality.”

Jake said that Nick’s arrest had impacted his grief further.

“I mean, he’s in jail, so,” Jake shared. “You know, I physically lost my parents, and then Nick was arrested. Now, it’s me and Romy.”

November 2026 Skywatching Guide: Meteors, Planets, Stars and a Full Moon

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

This month, you'll find three meteor showers and a planetary alignment starring four planets.

Cool nights, cool sights. November is the final full month of fall, and the sun sets even earlier now that daylight saving time has ended. That’ll give skygazers more time to see some cool sights without staying up horribly late or waking up unbearably early.

This month is incredibly active for amateur astronomers, with things to see as late as Nov. 30. While there aren’t any major celestial events, a series of smaller ones makes up for it.

Every planet is visible during the month of November, though depending on the time and day, you’ll get a better or worse view of them. Mercury and Venus are the most difficult to see due to their proximity to the sun.

Mercury: Mercury was at its eastern elongation during October, so it starts November a bit too close to the sun to see safely. It reaches western elongation on Nov. 20, when it’ll be easily visible just before sunrise for the rest of the month.

Venus: Venus starts the month just ahead of the sun at sunrise, technically making it possible to see every morning in November. It gets farther from the sun as the month goes on, making it easier and safer to see toward the end of the month.

Mars: Mars is high in the sky all month. It rises out of the eastern horizon shortly after midnight at the start of November and rises earlier as the month progresses. Early morning is the best time to see it, or right around midnight toward the end of the month.

Jupiter: Jupiter and Mars are close pals for all of November, rising at roughly the same time and right next to each other in the sky. The star Regulus joins in to form a trio, which is visible to the naked eye the entire month.

Saturn: Saturn is also visible every night in November. It’s in the eastern sky when the sun sets, and sets on the western horizon shortly before sunrise. Right after dark is the best time to see it, but you’ll need binoculars or a telescope to capture its glorious rings.

Neptune: Neptune and Saturn are teamed up for November, with both of them being very close in the sky and following a similar trajectory. Bring binoculars or a telescope.

Uranus: Uranus is in the night sky every day in November, rising in the east shortly before sunset and setting in the west right around sunrise. It reaches opposition on Nov. 25, which means it’s as far away from the sun as it can get, and will be the most visible. You’ll still want to bring some binoculars or a telescope for best results.

Most of the planets are visible in the night sky almost every night in November, but on Saturday, there’ll be a fun little planet parade. It’ll take place just before sunrise, so it’s mostly for early risers. Mercury, Venus, Mars and Jupiter are set to line up nicely, making them easy to spot. This is especially true for Jupiter and Mars, which should be right next to each other that night.

The show will start around Saturday and will last until around Nov. 25, depending on your location. The tricky part is catching Mercury in the sky before the sun comes up. It’s only about a 30-minute window since the sun closely follows Mercury up over the eastern horizon. The single best day to view it is Nov. 20, when Mercury is at its western elongation — as far from the sun as it can get.

Uranus is also visible at this time, bringing the number of visible planets to three. Saturn and Neptune are below the horizon. Uranus is on the other side of the sky, however, so it’s not technically part of the alignment.

If you miss the Southern Taurids, you only have to wait a week to catch a glimpse of the Northern Taurids meteor shower. This is another long-lasting meteor shower that started on Oct. 20 and runs until Dec. 10. It peaks on Wednesday evening and Thursday morning. The American Meteor Society notes that the moon is only 5% illuminated on Wednesday, giving viewers even better conditions than during the Southern Taurids.

It is otherwise virtually identical to the Southern Taurids. The shower appears to originate from the same constellation, Taurus, which rises and sets at almost exactly the same times as it did a week earlier. The Northern Taurids promise about five meteors per hour under optimal conditions, with an increased chance of fireballs. Both showers are even fueled by the same comet, 2P/Encke. It’s the space equivalent of a sitcom rerun airing just one week later.

Leonids is usually the most active meteor shower in November. It started on Nov. 6 and runs until Nov. 30, making it shorter than both Taurids meteor showers. It peaks on the evening of Nov. 16 and the morning of Nov. 17.

The good news is that the meteor shower is very easy to find. Meteors from it will appear to originate from the Leo constellation, which is right next to Mars and Jupiter, which are visible in the night sky.

The bad news is that this part of the sky doesn’t rise until around midnight, making this a meteor shower for night owls. It should produce about 15 meteors per hour under optimal conditions, though you can expect slightly fewer because the 41% illuminated moon will create enough natural light pollution to obscure fainter meteors.

The Leonids have produced exceptional meteor storms in the past, with hundreds of meteors per hour. That isn’t expected to happen in 2026, but the odds aren’t zero. They may improve after the comet loops around the sun again in 2031.

A micro moon is noticeably smaller and less bright than a supermoon or even a regular full moon.

November’s Beaver Moon takes place just two days before Thanksgiving. It is also the first of a string of four consecutive supermoons that will close out 2026 and begin 2027. This happens when the moon is within 90% of perigee — its closest point to Earth. The phenomenon makes the moon appear about 7% larger than a regular moon and 14% larger than a minimoon — and noticeably brighter than both.

Per The Old Farmer’s Almanac, the Beaver Moon reaches its peak illumination on Nov. 24 at 9:53 a.m. ET, which is after sunrise for most people in the US. That means the best time to see it is either the night before (Nov. 23) or the evening after (Nov. 24). The moon remains over 90% full from Nov. 22-27. If you plan on going skygazing, avoid those days like the plague, since the moon creates a ton of light pollution.

It’s not often that the same event happens twice in a single month, but it turns out Jupiter, Mars and the moon just can’t seem to get enough of each other. The trio closes out the month the same way it started: hanging out very close together in the night sky. And yes, Regulus will once again tag along.

Unlike the prior meetup, this one lasts for only one night before the moon dashes away. Since all four of them are visible with the naked eye and the moon is still mostly full, people shouldn’t have any trouble finding them. Eagle-eyed stargazers may see Algieba nearby as well. It’s the second brightest star in the Leo constellation (behind Regulus), and it’s also visible without binoculars or a telescope.

The constellations continue their summer-to-winter shakeup. Prominent constellations in the sky include Taurus, Pegasus, Capricornus, Aquila, Pisces and Aries. Mainstays such as the Big Dipper and Little Dipper are still out there, but can be difficult to see if you live too far south. As a rule, we recommend using a sky map or a tool such as Stellarium to pinpoint each object.

There are also some lesser-known asterisms to seek out, such as the Pleiades (the Seven Sisters), the Great Square of Pegasus and the Circlet of Pisces. The Summer Triangle is still visible, while the Winter Triangle becomes much easier to spot as it rises above the horizon earlier in the evening.

$13T Charles Schwab adds SOL, AVAX, and LINK as altcoins search for a bottom

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

If the trend does not reverse itself, the altcoin market could be heading into another capitulation phase.

Looking at this from a technical point of view, TOTAL2 is just 15% away from erasing all of its Q3 profits. This could put short-term holders in the red and continue to cause capital outflows. This is getting increasingly risky as Ethereum’s Fear and Greed Index has already fallen by nearly 60% in Q4. In short, the overall sentiment in the altcoin space is now looking weaker.

Against this backdrop, Charles Schwab’s latest announcement has given the altcoin sector a boost. The $13 trillion TradFi giant took to X to announce it has added Solana, Avalanche, and Chainlink to its crypto trading accounts, allowing eligible retail clients to buy and sell all three tokens directly.

Naturally, the bigger question is what this means for DeFi-TradFi integration.

With a vast network of around 40 million active brokerage accounts and an attractive 0.75% fee on crypto transactions, Charles Schwab offers easy trading functionality through Schwab.com, Mobile app, and Thinkorswim. While this is less of an institutional investment and more of a retail adoption milestone, it will make investing in altcoins much easier for traditional investors. This could increase the reach of altcoins and bring more retail liquidity into the market.

The key takeaway? The timing of Schwab’s move could hardly be better.

The altcoin market is looking for fresh liquidity and renewed institutional interest.

The stablecoin space has been struggling of late, with the overall market cap dropping below $300 billion. This week, Solana’s stablecoin market cap dropped by more than 4%, showcasing a loss of nearly $2 billion in liquidity. Other large altcoin ecosystems have also been struggling.

However, with Charles Schwab moving into altcoins, this may provide some much needed liquidity boost.

The altcoin sector is also going through an important phase. As mentioned previously, another crash could cause more capitulation, with TOTAL2 just 15% away from erasing all of its Q3 gains. However, trading volumes may be starting to normalize. In September, altcoin volume reached nearly 4x Bitcoin’s, hinting at heavy speculation. The latest cooldown could point to a healthier market structure.

In this context, Charles Schwab’s move could be more than just another step in TradFi-DeFi integration.

Instead, Schwab’s addition could create a new avenue for retail liquidity. Whilst it may not result in an instant revival, it will make SOL, AVAX and LINK more accessible, increasing retail involvement and helping revive a September-style wave of altcoin adoption.

Altcoins face more selling pressure as market sentiment and liquidity weaken.

Schwab’s move could bring fresh retail interest and liquidity into the altcoin market.

Polestar Formula 2030 Station Wagon Concept Offers a Futuristic Blueprint for Scandinavian Design

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Polestar’s head of design, Philipp Römers, is laying out the visual language of tomorrow for the all-electric brand.

Before Polestar’s public battle with the U.S. Department of Commerce earlier this year, the Swedish automaker was perhaps best known here for its avant-garde Scandinavian design language. Now, it seems like Polestar is simply teasing us after being banned from the United States starting in 2027, as the newly revealed Polestar Formula 2030 concept car is one of the coolest wagons we’ve ever seen.

The Formula 2030 concept car is the creation of Polestar’s head of design, Philipp Römers, and opens a window into the company’s future direction. The brand’s “remove the unnecessary, improve what remains, and refuse to compromise” design philosophy led the project, and Römers said the model is a practical four-door sports car. At 184 inches long, 79.5 inches wide, and with a 109-inch wheelbase, the Polestar Formula 2030 is sized between the outgoing Polestar 2 sedan and the incoming Polestar 7 hatchback. Polestar declined to provide any mechanical details about the Formula 2030.

“Design should always reflect the values and mission of a brand, and that is exactly what we did with Polestar Formula 2030 concept,” Römers said. “It is a physical embodiment of how we apply Polestar‘s core values of design, performance, and sustainability to the next production cars. A bold evolution of our already well-established design DNA to strengthen the brand‘s positioning with performance at its heart.”

A long hood, short overhangs, and 22-inch center-lock wheels exemplify the classic Polestar look, while also expressing “power and performance.” Dual-blade headlights mark the front end and serve as a preview of the updated Polestar 2, which will arrive in 2027. The shark nose and deep front splitter create the central spine of the Formula 2030 and give it a low and wide stance, adding to its performance credentials. A single large windscreen wiper pays homage to motorsport.

Polestar’s Single Blade rear lights highlight the rear end; instead of a connected light bar, the company wanted to explore new geometric shapes and angles. The rear glass also wraps around the side of the vehicle, opening a shallow side-view behind the C-pillar. A fixed rear wing sits on lit mounts, while the lower rear diffuser adds genuine downforce.

“We wanted to evolve, while respecting, the core design values we demonstrated with previous models that define the Polestar brand in such a recognizable way. And with Formula 2030, with its clear Polestar design attributes like the Dual Blade headlights, wraparound visor-like windshield and powerful haunches, we have done just that,” Nahum Escobedo, head of exterior design at Polestar, said. “But we’ve pushed perceptions, preconceptions, and the expected as well, with our considered choices in form language, amplification of the performance topic, and new detailing like the Single Blade rear lights.”

Inside the Formula 2030, the automaker stuck with minimalism. A 12.2-inch Center Screen Display is paired with the 20-inch “HyperDIM”—a precisely curved Driver Information Monitor—to round out the digital offerings. Air-inflatable bolsters are fitted to each seat to balance cushioned comfort and performance-oriented ergonomics. When combined with Polestar’s three drive modes—Performance, Normal, and Autonomous—the center displays and adjustable seats will position themselves for the mood; for example, in Performance mode, the steering wheel, screens, and seats will lower and move toward the driver. Similarly, Polestar will offer a configurable mix of physical and digital controls in the center stack; users can choose which controls they want to be actioned by the physical buttons. (Attentive readers will also notice a steering yoke in place of the typical steering wheel.)

Finally, the Polestar Formula 2030 shows off a new “Nitrogen” exterior color, which is specific to the concept. Variations of the new color in gloss, matte, and satin finishes are scattered around the car. Polestar’s characteristic Swedish Gold color adorns the Brembo brake calipers, valve caps, interior detailing, and seat belts. The interior finish of the doors is made from a fully recyclable material, which is contrasted by exposed structural interior parts such as the seat frame made from low-emission galvanized steel. All told, the Polestar Formula 2030 offers a compelling glimpse of what’s ahead for the Swedish automaker, and we’re sure sad to miss out on it.

A New York transplant hailing from the Pacific Northwest, Emmet White has a passion for anything that goes: cars, bicycles, planes, and motorcycles. After learning to ride at 17, Emmet worked in the motorcycle industry before joining Autoweek in 2022 and Road & Track in 2024. The woes of alternate side parking have kept his fleet moderate, with a 2014 Volkswagen Jetta GLI and a BMW 318i E30 street parked in his Queens community.

Highly active Russian volcano plays peek-a-boo with an astronaut

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A 2019 astronaut photo shows the near-perfect cone of a highly active volcano poking its heavily scarred and partially snow-covered head above a thick bank of low-lying clouds over the Kuril Islands.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Where is it? Atlasov Island, off the coast of Russia's Kamchatka Peninsula [50.84755357, 155.56126752]

What's in the photo? The summit of Alaid volcano peeking above a thick layer of low-lying clouds

Who took the photo? An unnamed astronaut on board the International Space Station

This eye-catching astronaut photo shows a towering, highly active Russian volcano poking its head above a thick bank of swirling clouds over the Kuril Islands.

The lofty peak is located on Atlasov Island, the northernmost of the Kuril Islands — a chain of roughly 50 largely uninhabited volcanic landmasses stretching along a fault line that runs between Russia and Japan in the Sea of Okhotsk. Altasov lies 40 miles (65 kilometers) off Russia's Kamchatka Peninsula, which is also renowned for its volcanic activity.

The island consists mainly of a single, massive volcano, called Alaid, which is known for its near-perfect cone shape. Alaid reaches up to 7,497 feet (2,285 meters) above sea level, making it the tallest peak in the Kuril Islands.

Alaid has erupted at least nine times in the past 50 years, including a major outburst in April 1981 that ejected a 1,250-mile-long (2,000 km) plume of thick smoke and ash into the atmosphere, according to the Smithsonian Institution's Global Volcanism Program.

Its most recent eruption occurred between September and November 2022, when a river of lava flowed down its flank (see below). During this outburst, the volcano also spit out smoke and ash that was spotted from space alongside another eruption plume coming from the nearby island Paramushir.

Alaid last erupted in November 2022 and unleashed a scorching lava flow down its snow-covered slopes. This photo also shows what Atlasov Island looks like when it is not obscured by clouds.

In the astronaut's photo, roughly half of Atlasov Island rises above a deep, low-lying bank of swirling clouds. The summit of Alaid, crowned by a roughly mile-wide (1.6 km) caldera, is also obscured by clouds. (The volcano was not erupting when the photo was taken.)

Farther down the volcano's flanks are a series of snow-filled gullies carved out by lava flows from previous eruptions. Eagle-eyed viewers may also be able to spot some parasitic cones — small openings where lava burst through in the past — around the edge of the cloud layer, according to NASA's Earth Observatory.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

The area surrounding a cluster of parasitic cones on the volcano's northwest flank (top right in the photo) is devoid of vegetation ‪—‬ a result of another major eruption in 1972, which scoured the surrounding landscape with hot ash. A 2009 study revealed fresh signs of recovery in the affected area, but noted that it could take hundreds of years for vegetation to return to pre-eruption levels.

The clouds are not the only thing the island rises above. Alaid is essentially the summit of a much larger underwater volcano that lies beneath the Sea of Okhotsk. The rest of the island has grown around the exposed cone as lava flows have reached sea level and solidified. Millions of years ago, this larger volcano was likely connected to the Kamchatka Peninsula via a land bridge that has since been submerged.

In 1981, a major eruption caused Alaid to unleash a massive plume of smoke and ash that reached up to 1,250 miles long.

Astronauts and satellites have snapped several other stunning shots of intriguing landmasses nearby, including a quartet of end-to-end volcanoes on Simushir Island and a set of volcanic "nesting dolls" on Onekotan Island.

And in 2022, the Landsat 8 satellite also spotted the iconic summit of Mount Vesuvius peeking through a gap in the clouds over Italy, just like Alaid.

A 2024 astronaut photo shows the icy surface of Pangong Lake breaking apart in the lofty Tibetan Plateau. The point where the ice meets the water almost perfectly matches a disputed border between China and India.

A 2020 astronaut photo shows the unique structure of Ol Doinyo Lengai, an active stratovolcano in Tanzania that was recently adorned with a bright white "ash cone." The explosive peak produces a rare type of lava that is unusually cool but travels remarkably quickly.

A 2010 satellite photo shows the point where a small lake bisects the snowy rim of an ancient glacier on Canada's Baffin Island. The rippling, snow-rimmed structure is the last remaining fragment of a colossal ice sheet that once covered large parts of North America.

He covers a wide range of topics including space exploration, planetary science, space weather, climate change, animal behavior and paleontology. Man United chiefs set for Monaco summit TOMORROW to decide Michael Carrick's fate... with manager taking training today despite facing the sack

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

By CHRIS WHEELER, NORTHERN SPORTS WRITER and NATHAN SALT - FOOTBALL REPORTER

Published: 02:40 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 09:07 EST, 10 November 2026

Michael Carrick’s fate as Manchester United head coach is set to be decided on Wednesday when chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox meet with minority owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe in Monaco.

Berrada and Wilcox will make their recommendation to Ratcliffe, with all the indications pointing to Carrick being sacked after United dropped into the relegation zone.

It’s understood that the Old Trafford hierarchy would want to inform Carrick of their decision face to face, so there may not be any confirmation from the club until Thursday.

The 45-year-old turned up for work at Carrington at 7.15am on Tuesday to oversee training with the players who are not away on international duty amid increasing uncertainty over his future.

United sources maintain that a final decision has yet to be made, with the club reluctant to engage in a running commentary over Carrick's position.

The manager's position increasingly precarious after they dropped into the relegation zone despite salvaging a 4-4 draw against Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday.

It left him with just one win from ten Premier League games this season – United’s worst start since Sir Alex Ferguson replaced Ron Atkinson 40 years ago.

Carrick is taking training as usual this morning - with a cut-down group of players as a number of United's stars have departed on international duty.

Michael Carrick is pictured arriving at Manchester United's training ground at 7.15am

Mauricio Pochettino is the frontrunner to replace Carrick - leaving his role as US head coach

Who do you think is truly to blame for United¿s disastrous season - the manager or the club¿s leadership?

Mauricio Pochettino has emerged as a new frontrunner to replace Carrick. The Argentine is currently in charge of the US national team, but his contract includes a £7million release clause.

Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann, who were both under consideration when United gave Carrick more time to turn the season around following a 3-0 home defeat by Bournemouth last month, could also be in contention.

In the three games since then, Carrick’s side lost at Chelsea, staged a remarkable comeback to beat Roma in the Champions League in midweek and then fought back again to draw with Villa.

Although it’s understood that no final decision has been reached over Carrick’s future and insiders say that speculation over his sacking is premature, the expectation within Old Trafford is that a new man will be in charge for the visit to Anfield a week on Sunday.

Carrick replaced Ruben Amorim in mid-January and the former United midfielder was handed a two-year contract in May after guiding United to a third-place finish and qualification for the Champions League, losing just two of his 17 games as head coach.

But this season has unravelled alarmingly for United with his only two wins in 15 games in all competitions coming at home to newly promoted Ipswich and Sabah of Azerbaijan.

Asked after Sunday’s chaotic draw with Villa if he would consider quitting, a defiant Carrick said: ‘That's not going to happen.

‘We’re in November. I don’t like this situation and where we are. But we can do something about it. I'm quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in, and it's got to win games.’

Avram Glazer and Sir Jim Ratcliffe, co-owners, are pictured at Old Trafford in September

Sacking Carrick would leave United searching for their eighth permanent manager or head coach since Ferguson retired 13-and-a-half years ago.

Pochettino was strongly linked with the job when Erik ten Hag was appointed in 2022 and again when the Dutchman was sacked two years later.

The 54-year-old has extensive Premier League experience at Tottenham, Chelsea and Southampton and was also in charge at Paris Saint-Germain.

Howe was previously seen as the favourite but it’s understood there has been no contact between United and the former Newcastle boss.

It would cost United around £3million in compensation to appoint the 48-year-old under the terms of his departure from St James’s Park in July.

Nagelsmann is available after leaving his role as head coach of the German national team in the summer when they were eliminated from the World Cup following a penalty shootout defeat by Paraguay in the round of 32.

Report: Liverpool take first step towards signing amazing Premier League star

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have taken step number one towards signing a Premier League star. The Reds are in some sort of contact about getting a deal done.

It's November so we're officially at the stage where Liverpool are sounding out genuine transfer targets ahead of January. It's this period - especially with the international break - that clubs figure out who they could actually move for.

Liverpool are in a strange sport with that, however. The Reds made some massive moves in the summer, which usually means zero January business, but they're also just not good enough.

Is the solution actually spending even more money? Possibly. It depends on what you believe the problem to be. Liverpool certainly won't blame Andoni Iraola at this stage, so a weak, imbalanced squad will likely be perceived as the culprit.

And that's why they've already made an approach.

Liverpool make Pedro Porro approachTeamTalk reports that Liverpool have inquired about Pedro Porro. The right-back is expected to have a lot of interest in him this winter and there appears to be half a chance that Tottenham Hotspur sell. Again, though, the Reds aren't alone. Barcelona, Real Madrid, Chelsea and Manchester City would all like Porro, too. All can easily claim that they'd make use of him, too. But maybe Liverpool have the most need. They're using Ronald Araujo at right-back - a player who was actually signed to be a centre-back. The latter is undoubtedly his natural position, too, but a lack of faith in Jeremie Frimpong coupled with Conor Bradley's injury has forced the Uruguayan to the flank.

TeamTalk reports that Liverpool have inquired about Pedro Porro. The right-back is expected to have a lot of interest in him this winter and there appears to be half a chance that Tottenham Hotspur sell.

Again, though, the Reds aren't alone. Barcelona, Real Madrid, Chelsea and Manchester City would all like Porro, too. All can easily claim that they'd make use of him, too.

But maybe Liverpool have the most need. They're using Ronald Araujo at right-back - a player who was actually signed to be a centre-back. The latter is undoubtedly his natural position, too, but a lack of faith in Jeremie Frimpong coupled with Conor Bradley's injury has forced the Uruguayan to the flank.

Signing a natural right-back is almost certainly on the agenda, then. Potentially for big money, too, and Porro is one of the best possible options to upgrade the position.

But with this much competition, is the price likely to be 'good'? We're not so sure. Spurs won't actually want to sell, even if there's a chance, and we imagine this will approach £60m+.

Then we're in questionable territory. Liverpool don't require a right-back that badly, do they? Not when a new midfielder almost certainly improves the squad more.
